    Lightning Strikes

    Chapter Summary

    A storm brews. Damage is dealt.

    Chapter Notes

    Chapter Four: Lightning Strikes

     

    The rain had begun while the sun was still out.

    It started with a cool and refreshing wind through the fields, which then pushed over the rolling hills with a bit more insistence. Lena delighted in seeing the smiling faces of her workers as they stopped mid-task and stood up straight to brace the unseasonably cool breeze. Another bizarre southernism, Lena assumed, though however unspoken, they all seemed to be of the same mind. No matter what was happening, everyone would stop for the momentary respite that was a sweeping wind. It whistled a long chorus through the vines, and not long after that, brought with it a gentle rain. There was something about a daylight rain that was just as much a foreboding as it was awesome. Lena couldn’t believe her eyes.

    But then the skies soon faded to a deep green, and everyone was suddenly rushing inside. Lena took up the large chain of the rolling bay door and pulled with all of her might to bring it up. Two men jumped to help her, and before she knew it, her feet were flat upon the ground again once the door began to move. They chuckled at her beneath the rolling thunder and tried ushering her inside, but Lena wouldn’t be caught dead taking shelter until she knew that everyone else was safe first.

    Rain was lashing against the side of the building now at a sideways angle. The storm had come upon them quickly. Much to her relief, Lena saw Kara running behind a small group from the furthest reach of the plot they were to work that day. What vines they had yet to pick might suffer from such a torrential downpour, but that was the last thing on Lena’s mind. The way the sky had amassed with such a dark and baleful nature had her wholly concerned. In the past, any time Shade Tree Cellars had been at the mercy of a thunderstorm, Lena had always been inside, safe and sound in the covers of her own bed. Seeing its grand sense of mercilessness now in the light of day shook her to the bone.

    The swollen clouds had crawled quickly across the sky, flashing intermittent and bright deep inside their heavy blue-green pockets. Purple and white cracked across them with bitter bolts of lightning, and the first thunderclap had Lena leaping further inside by the time Kara had run in.

    Everyone came to be huddled under the catwalked chutes, as far from the rolling bay door as they could get. The rain was unable to reach so far inside but there was still the risk of flying debris. Lena would not at all be surprised if the flash storm swept itself up into several tornados. Twisters, they would call them, as they twisted themselves up from the ground— if served the right conditions. The danger was above just as much as it was below. Kara pulled the last man by the back of his belt away from the rolling door as he tried to bring it back down. A rectangular piece of tin roof from one of the tool sheds nearby had landed there in his absence only a second later, giving out a great and awful -clang- sound.

    Thunder boomed overhead, and Kara joked with everyone else as she pushed them further inside with long sweeps of her arms. How could she be so calm at a time like this? Lena only wished to join in and laugh, but her anxiousness had long set in. It was so loud. The rain, the thunder, the scrape of hand tools along the paved drive… Seemingly satisfied, Kara backpedaled to speak with Lena, looking over her shoulder as she went. There sat her sunbeam of a smile upon her face as if it were any other day. It washed a small wave of relief over Lena just to see it. A beacon of light in her stormiest of days.

    Kara was quick to read the apprehension in Lena’s expression. She held a hand out to grasp gently at her shoulder, “Are you okay?”

    “Everything’s fine.” Lena replied with a nod, as if she were not only trying to convince Kara, but herself as well.

    “It’ll be over in a few minutes, there’s—”

    Suddenly, there came a great and terrifying -boom- from a particularly close lightning strike. The loudest thing Lena had ever heard in her entire life; She wasn’t sure at first, but it rang quite like a bell in her head. The crowd of nearly twenty heads all ducked at once. Some cupped their ears, but everyone went quiet.

    That’s when Lena realized… It had been a bell.

    Lightning had struck the villa’s belltower, and the impact (and resulting ringing sound) had traveled all the way to the other side, rumbling deep into all of the very old stone and repaired brick.

    What Lena hadn’t realized, though, was that she’d shot straight into Kara’s arms.

    They had wrapped around her tightly without a single ounce of hesitance. Lena could feel the side of her face where her cheek was pressed into the rain-soaked collar of Kara’s shirt, unbuttoned slightly from the neck down like always. Time stood still. Leaving her cheek there to graze against the warm flush of Kara’s skin was like emitting lightning of her own; incandescent and jarring for how badly she wished for the storm to go on. But time, it seemed, was content just to move on without her.

    Everyone seemed to loosen up again, and the sounds of joking laughter resumed. Kara and Lena split apart.

    “That was intense…”

    Kara shoved her hands deep into her pockets with a smile. “I’ll go take a peek up there after the rain lets up.”

    “Are you sure?”

    “Yeah,” Kara nodded, “I’ll take care of it.”

     

     

    Just like that, the rain had gone.

    The sun was shining again, and the heady smell of earth and damp grass filled Lena’s nose. Truly, the breaking of the storm was pleasant enough, but the lingering humidity was enough to make the outside bake like an oven. A cool stint beneath the blowing a/c of the Shade Tree Bar & Grill was close at hand. Lena smiled into the stark difference of dry, chilled air once she stepped inside. A bite for lunch might help to settle some of her nerves.

    There were customers at her tables, and all was well in the world once again.

    “Ouch!”

    …Or perhaps that was just wishful thinking.

    The distinct sound of a pained shriek had come from the kitchen all of a sudden, and Lena quirked her chin with a slight curiosity there, wondering if their maître d’ was having some sort of difficulty. It had definitely sounded like Nia, at least. Rather than park her tired feet at the bar like she did almost every early afternoon, Lena took herself through the swinging double doors and noticed the lights flickering with some agitation upon the other side, as if a switch was bouncing consistently between its on and off positions.

    Nia was cautioning Brainy where he stood at the range, motioning towards his hands as if he ought not to touch anything else. He did, though, and as soon as his fingers lit upon one of the stovetop range’s knobs, he leapt backwards with a perturbed grunt like it had come up and bit him.

    “Woah—” Lena went to cross the kitchen floor. Nia quickly held her hands up and begged for her to stop.

    “Wait- don’t touch anything!” She said.

    “What do you mean?”

    “I don’t know, the electricity’s really weird in here for some reason.” Nia unknowingly placed her hand flat upon the end of a metal prep counter then, one that had plug outlets for tabletop mixers and other appliances, and she almost immediately realized her mistake with the sound of a sharp -zap-. She gripped tightly around her wrist and then warned Lena again, “Seriously, don’t touch anything.”

    “Not good.” Lena replied with a stiff raise of her brow. She gestured for them both to follow her out of the kitchen after that, “Come on. Let’s shut down until we can get an electrician out here.”

    “Kara’s good with wiring.” Nia offered on their way out.

    “I’m sure she is, but I’d rather have a master electrician take the risk instead of my foreman.”

    “Aww…”

    Lena glanced over at Nia’s proud little pout right about then. She found herself wondering if she knew… But what was there to know? Lena had taken great care not to show favoritism— Alright, that was a lie. Everyone knew she and Kara got along perfectly well, but Lena was beginning to wonder if the sight of them together would soon swirl into rumor. She couldn’t have that.

    Even though a tiny little part of her wanted it. Badly.

    “Take the names and numbers of the guests sitting over there and offer them a discounted meal for whenever we re-open.”

    “On it!” Nia went out from behind the bar with a happy -click- of her heels.

    Oh, God… Lena thought. That one’s going to catch on fast if you’re not more careful.

     

     

    Idle hands were the devil’s playthings.

    Or so Lena had heard…

    She found herself staring at her phone more times than she’d like to admit, blank screen awaiting the input of text in a message to one Kara Danvers. Lena backed out of the message each time, heart pounding with her temptation just to ask Kara to come up to the villa for a drink.

    Instead, she made her way down the long, stone stairwell towards the underground vat room. It was nearly time to close their doors for the evening. The only people left on the winery grounds were down below with her, tank operators and the like. They held specialized degrees in winemaking and stirred the fermenting must as needed, adding sulfur dioxide from time to time to prevent the growth of harmful bacteria or to stop the yeast-making process altogether. Less than half a dozen white coats and hairnets shuffled from one end of the industrial-sized room to the other, but Lena was lost in thought yet again…

    Why don’t I just tell her?

    Would that make it easier? Or worse?

    Lena sighed.

    Where to even begin?

    Oh, this is so inappropriate…

    What about Jack?

    She squinted for a moment then at a temperature dial where it jutted out beside a large vat. The tank itself was a monstrosity, reaching nearly all the way up to the ceiling. A second network of catwalks was where she saw more white coats as they peered down into the fermenting grapes. The dial was off by several degrees, noting the temperature to be much too cool during this stage. Lena puzzled at it closer and then startled at the sudden flickering of the bright, fluorescent lights from above. She looked upwards, confused.

    It wasn’t long after that before she began to feel a bit woozy.

    The floor seemed to come up and down in front of her like waves breaking upon a shoreline. Lena shook her head with an almost drunk sort of feeling.

    What the hell…?

    A -thud- came from above her head then, and she looked up once more to stare at the underside of the grated catwalk. Someone had fallen, and they were just lying there…

    Lena couldn’t seem to keep her feet underneath herself by that point. She gripped at the sleeves of her own coat and pulled it free from her arms. It had somehow become so very heavy, and was pulling her down, down, down…

    “Help—” The word seemed to lag moments out from her mouth after she’d said it. The color of steel and stone began to blend together after that. Lena crawled on her hands and knees, fighting to pull in one breath at a time. The air felt thick like mud, and she couldn’t breathe.

    That was when the alarm went off. It strobed red against the drooping lids of her eyes.

    No.

    No no no…

    Lena fell upon her face finally, unable to do anything else but watch the spotty edges of her vision as it turned dark. It was terrifying; the noise, and the spinning, and the way the skin upon her cheekbones tingled from a lack of oxygenated blood…

    “Help.” She whispered again, using the last of her breath.

    Then, everything went dark.

    Much to her surprise (or confusion), she could still hear somehow, and felt that her body was being moved. The unmistakable booted -kick- against a door seemed to echo between her ears. She wasn’t sure if it had come before or after. Then it was her name, shouted over and over again… and somebody pawing frantically around her face and neck…

    Lena eventually felt the cool touch of tile against her shoulder blades, and then the warm rush of air as it made its way into her lungs again. Her cheeks began to tingle once more, and then she could feel her lips.

    Someone else’s were upon them.

    There was the taste of salt, wet along her lips like the feeling of tears, and then the sound of her name again. Over and over and over… Lena opened her eyes and felt as though she’d stared straight into the sun. She tried blinking rapidly through her blurred double vision.

    No, she realized. Not the sun.

    …Kara.

    A single tear track had run out from the corner of each of her eyes. She was scared. Something Lena had never seen before: Kara Danvers, strong like an ox and fearless in the face of powerful forces of nature… scared absolutely to death.

    “Lena—” She panted, still hovering over Lena’s supine frame, “Oh, my God. Can you breathe? Breathe, please…”

    Lena nodded while she caught her breath. The ringing in her ears seemed to drown out most of everything else: the still blaring alarm, and the running feet beside her as they went down the stairwell and towards the vat room. Kara called out to them as they ran past.

    “Masks! Somebody force those shutters open; I don’t care if you have to break them apart board by board!”

    “What happened…?” Lena went to ask. Kara looked back down and unbuttoned the rest of her own shirt just to take it and ball it up into a pillow. It went beneath Lena’s head as soon as she tried asking again, “Kara, what—”

    “The CO2 alarm- the storm must have messed with the scrubbers, or something. I don’t know.”

    Lena’s next thought went immediately to the rest of her team down below, “Wait, there’s—”

    Kara stopped her with a hand upon her shoulder when she went to sit up. Lena was still so very loopy; it made the upstairs walls of the winery almost undulate in front of her. She blinked rapidly to regain her bearings again, and Kara hushed over her for a moment…

    “Don’t worry. Everything’s gonna be fine.”

    There was that wonderful face of hers again; The one Lena had mistaken for the sun. Before she even knew what was happening, Lena’s hands had possessed a mind of their own again and went to grip at the thin cotton collar of Kara’s white undershirt. She made fists there and pulled, and suddenly, Kara’s lips were upon her own again. Lena sighed with the feeling of it. She’d almost died, for God’s sake… If there was ever a time that she would give in to the lust which she harbored deep within her bones, it would have been now. She didn’t care. Kara had stared at those same lips many a morning, noon, and night. Life had been forced back into her. If not for Kara, then she surely would have winded up dead. Lena owed her everything.

    They kissed longer than either of them realized, apparently. Certainly long enough for one of Lena’s hands to filter through the spectacular flax-color of Kara’s hair and to hold firm there at the back of her head. No one else was around to see... Not that Lena cared at the time. They finally parted with weary, half-lidded eyes, and Lena’s better sense of propriety slowly came crashing back.

    She loosed her hand from the back of Kara’s head and then apologized, “I… I’m sorry.”

    “It’s alright.” Kara nodded. The corners of her mouth seemed to want to turn up with a smile, but instead she pursed them into a thin line. “You don’t have to apologize.”

    At the very least, Lena thought that she should thank her. “Thank you,” She said, “…for…”

    Kara finally smiled. “Listen, I’m just glad I was around to hear the alarm.”

    “God, me too.”

     

     

    The lightning strike had indeed run afoul of the villa’s network of electrical wiring.

    One of the dangers of winemaking was all of the off-gassing during the fermentation process. The release of excess carbon dioxide posed a greater risk for underground facilities, but thankfully, the villa itself was old enough to still make use of secondary ventilation shafts up towards the surface. Kara’s thorough inspecting of the grounds, as well as what lied below, had saved lives.

    Lena was grateful, far more than words could say, but she was also horrified. She could see the lawsuits now: negligence, assault…

    “Oh, no…” Her head went into her hands.

    She’d kissed Kara.

    Flashes of blue and white painted the outside walls near the winery with the arrival of fire engines and paramedics alike. Left to wallow in her growing guilt, Lena was sat at the open back of an ambulance, blanket over her shoulders for comfort. Four others had been down in the vat room with her when the carbon dioxide filtering system happened to disengage. The villa itself was so large, it was impossible to tell where the extent of the lightning damage had reached. Nobody knew until it was almost too late.

    Lena took a moment to squint through the many colored lights of emergency response vehicles towards a familiar looking woman. She had chin length auburn hair and looked almost exactly like the woman who had made such a swift and angry exit back at Kara’s trailer home. Lena stood up and disrobed from her blanket in order to get a closer look. There was Kara standing beside the woman, who Lena could see clearly now was an EMT by profession. They were speaking animatedly with their hands at one another. Kara happened to notice her first.

    “Lena—” Kara stood up straight from where she was leaned slightly against the back of another bus, “This is my sister, Alex.”

    “Hello…”

    “Sign here.” The woman named Alex shoved a clipboard with some release forms jammed into it near Lena’s chest. She held a stern, almost decidedly unhappy expression upon her face. Lena took up the pen from her other hand and signed without objection. Alex then took a step back and slammed the half door of the bus a bit hard— perhaps to emphasize her poor mood. “Fix your CO2 scrubbers.”

    Then, with turn and a huff, she was gone.

    Kara broke out with a sheepish sort of grin, displaying the full, pretty rows of her teeth, “She sort of runs a bit hot, sometimes.” She explained with a shrug, “I don’t imagine she was thrilled whenever she got the call.”

    “Kara, I’m so sorry,” Lena apologized again, “I don’t mean to cause your family any more distress—”

    Kara gave small waves of her hands, “No, no, you’re fine. Things happen. She’s…” Then she pinched at her chin for a moment in thought, “Overprotective might be a good way of putting it.”

    “I’m just really glad you’re okay.”

    “Me?” Kara scoffed. “You almost died…”

    “But thanks to you, I didn’t.”

    A spell of silence seemed to fall between them after that. Kara ground the heel of her boot once again into the pebbled dirt, just like she always did whenever she was just a shade nervous, or embarrassed. In any case, Lena knew that it was because of her. She wanted so badly to bring up their kiss, or her kissing Kara, rather, but she chose just to enjoy their shared silence for now. Maybe they would never talk about it. That was a thought that Lena hated.

    There came some shouting from nearby then, and both of them turned at once to see where it had come from. Jack dashed from one firetruck to another, gripping one pair of shoulders at a time and hollering all the while for Lena.

    “Where is she?!” He cried out. As soon as he broke through the small throng of paramedics standing in his way, he saw her. “Lena—!” Jack made another mad dash towards her and then slid to a clumsy stop.

    “I’m fine—” Lena said.

    Jack hadn’t heard her. He’d grabbed her up as soon as he could, squeezing her and kissing at the side of her face until he could calm himself some and speak again. “I had no idea- I was away on—”

    “Where have you been, Jack?” Lena shoved him lightly. She looked back to her left and found that Kara had made herself scarce. Quickly, too. She was gone, and Lena instantly felt mad. It was a good thing that Jack hadn’t been present for once, to be sure, but for whatever reason that Lena couldn’t quite name, she realized that she resented the mere sight of him now. The scent of alcohol quickly filled the space between them. “Are you drunk?” She asked.

    “I- I’ve been at a business meeting—”

    “All day? Conducting business, I’m sure.”

    “Lena…” Jack began to wither. It only made her angrier. “My darling. Please don’t be upset, if I had known—”

    “Don’t.” Lena took a step back from him whenever his arms went to wrap around her again. “You didn’t know, because big surprise, you weren’t here.”

    “Aren’t you glad that I wasn’t though? What’s the matter with you?” He asked defensively, “I thought I’d lost you. We’re to be married soon, I just want for—”

    “That’s it, Jack. Stop.” Lena let him stumble forward slightly. It did pain her to be so resentful towards him, but she couldn’t stand it any longer. She wanted the Jack that was her best friend back. Not this puffed up, drunken peacock of a man who seemed to be just as concerned for himself as he claimed to be for her. “That’s it.” She said again with a shake of her head, “I can’t do this. I can’t marry you.”

    Jack’s lower jaw seemed to travel up and down for a few moments. “What are you talking about?”

    “I don’t want to marry you.”

    “Sweetheart—”

    “I’m not your sweetheart, Jack!” Lena gave an adamant stamp of her foot as she tried her best to keep her voice down, “You only began to call me things like that whenever my parents put the idea of us marrying into your head. Did you ever stop to consider my feelings on the matter?”

    “I—”

    “No, you didn’t.” Lena hissed. “I suppose it’s partly my fault as well, I didn’t exactly object. Not loudly enough, anyways.”

    “Lena, please.”

    “No.” She shook her head again.

    “Let’s talk about this tomorrow, please, or whenever you’re—”

    “We can talk, but my feelings won’t change.”

    Jack hung his head with a sigh, and then he bobbed it up and down dejectedly. “Alright.”

    “I’m going inside. Go home, Jack.”

    “Will you call me tomorrow?” He begged, eyes lifted up and glistening with tears. The sight of him crying only made her think of how he must have felt sorry for himself, and not her. It took everything just to part ways from him with a modicum of grace.

    “I don’t know. I’d like to be alone for a while.”

    “Okay…” He replied with a defeated droop of his head once again.

    Lena’s guilt only began to wax full whenever she finally found herself within the safe environs of her bedroom. She tossed each of the decorative pillows from the head of her bed onto the floor and then each piece of her clothing along with them. She scrambled just to bring her legs beneath her fluffy comforter as fast as humanly possible. She wanted to shower, to wash herself clean of her sins, but she hadn’t the strength to take herself up from her bed in order to get there. She felt so awful for the way that she’d yelled at Jack, and for what had happened earlier that night. For nearly killing the four others that were with her in the vat room, and for the kiss that she’d foisted upon Kara after the woman had gone out of her way to save her…

    Lena wrapped her hands as far as they would go around her ribs once she began to sob, but no matter how tightly she hugged herself, it still wasn’t what she wanted.

    It was who she wanted that mattered…

    More than anything in the world.

    …And she couldn’t have her.

    Chapter End Notes

    Despite Everything

    Chapter Summary

    Shade Tree Cellars hosts a party.

    Chapter Notes

    Chapter Five: Despite Everything

     

    Lena was thinking about kissing Kara.

    Naturally… As one does whenever one unintentionally kisses someone else for the first time… Oh, yes. She thought about it.

    She thought about it a lot.

    Though the blinds were drawn over her long office window, Lena had turned them open just enough in order to peek through. The subject of her staring stood there upon the other side, hoisting bundles of firewood off the back of a delivery truck one at a time.

    Was it ever Lena’s good fortune that the delivery driver seemed to have gotten himself lost (an easy thing to do upon so much land, the small roads of the vineyard weren’t exactly posted with signs) and decided to unload there at the very front, right in front of Lena’s office? Kara’s arms were simply a thing of beauty.

    They were sculpted quite like a man’s but still feminine in their own right. Tanned from shoulder to slender fingertip, Lena’s mouth seemed to have an awful habit of vacating itself of all moisture whenever she laid her eyes upon them. There she sat at her desk chair; eyes following Kara’s every move from behind the safety of her window blinds. She swallowed thickly and smiled whenever Kara had apparently said something funny. Those who were floating around outside in an effort to help were all suddenly laughing, so it must have been her effortless sense of humor, no doubt. Kara seemed to have a way with words alright, in the way that she was sharp as a tack and quick like the crack of a whip. Lena admired her for her humor, her patience, her work ethic, her smile, her strength…

    …her body…

    Again, her mind drifted to whence she was mouth to mouth with her sunny savior; her lips heavily slanted with a faint glimmer of a moan trapped behind them. She’d not thought to introduce her tongue during the moment, since it had happened so spontaneously and without warning, but Lena imagined what it might have felt like to have Kara’s tongue in her mouth. Somehow from there it would draw downward and towards her neck… and then her navel… and then right between her legs…

    The door to her office came swinging open, and Lena’s hand darted out from behind her blazer where she’d felt along at her breasts.

    “I’ve had the greatest idea—!”

    “Nia!”

    “Oop.” Nia quickly swallowed her intrusion with a “sorry…” and then she took a step back through the door’s threshold to knock politely there upon it with an apologetic grin.

    Lena had made herself up quickly. At least her back had been facing the door, for heaven’s sake. She turned around in her chair and pulled the long, impossibly straight midnight color of her hair behind an ear. “Yes?”

    Nia cleared her throat and then went to step inside again, bouncing upon her heels like before. “I have an idea that’ll bring loads of people into the bar.”

    Lena nodded. Nia didn’t normally sit whenever she met with Lena in her office— even though there were two perfectly good chairs to do just that. She was always so excitable. A glass half-full kind of personality, and very brief in the way of her comings and goings. There was always so much to do, apparently. Lena pulled her lips into a smile and sighed through her nose, “Alright. Let’s hear it.”

    “Jeremiah’s birthday is this weekend, and the rec center still hasn’t fixed their roof, so if it rains, everyone will get wet, and I already had plans for a cake—”

    “Nia, Nia,” Lena waved her down, “Darling, you’re beginning at the end again. What is it you’re saying?”

    “Oh, right. Jeremiah’s birthday! We could have it here, at the bar.”

    “And… who is Jeremiah?”

    Nia gave a -snap- of her fingers near her head as if she had meant to start there. “Kara’s dad- I mean, our- Eh, it's a long story... But he’s kind of a big deal in town. Everyone comes out for Jeremiah’s birthday… and I mean everyone.”

    Lena went to brush the backs of her knuckles lightly beneath her chin. Kara hadn’t spoken much at all about her father except for the fact that he was sick. Lena hadn’t possessed the heart (or the courage) to ask about anything else. There was that very clear (or fuzzy) boundary between them, still. One that smacked of “employer” and “employee”.

    “Okay.” Lena considered it. “Could you—”

    “Handle everything? Of course!”

    “I don’t suppose you’ve already run this by Kara, have you?”

    “I—” Nia rambled to a halt and then stuck a finger up in the air, “I will. Immediately. Soon. Right away.”

    Lena laughed. “You know how much I admire your initiative. Go crazy.”

    “Yes…” Nia pumped a fist with her excitement.

    “I’m serious, though. If Kara’s keen on the idea, then you have my blessing. Just keep me updated. I’d hate to show up late.”

    “Of course!” Nia nearly ran into the door for having turned around so fast, “You can count on me!”

     

     

    Lena had never seen the inside of Shade Tree Bar & Grill so full before.

    The wine bar was seated from end to end, and every table throughout was filled. People even stood at the back with their trucker hats and work gloves still in hand. It seemed that everyone had pressed pause on their day just to come out and celebrate one man. Apparently, he was a man worth celebrating. Friends and neighbors had done nothing but talk about Jeremiah Danvers from the moment they began to arrive.

    Kara’s dad had seen the town of Marble Falls through the hardest of times. He offered shelter to those in need and fed those who weren’t able to feed themselves. He apparently raised many children, though he fathered only one of his own— A surprise to Lena’s ears, since Kara had already introduced her to her sister. It had never occurred to her that Alex may have been adopted. They appeared more than anything like birds of a feather, at least in their mannerisms from the few short interactions Lena had seen. She didn’t ask and simply moved on to the next table to share a warm welcome with more guests. Everyone had smiles upon their faces, and that’s when it struck her… The vineyard suddenly felt more like a home to Lena than it ever had before. Its missing ingredient had finally rose to the surface… Love filled these walls.

    As if on cue, everyone turned their faces to the front door wherein a rolling wheelchair entered. Alex pushed her father inside and before anyone could get a word in, people had started to clap. Lena felt a chill race beneath her skin as the sound of clapping grew louder, and then a cheer went out whenever Jeremiah stood up. He’d done so slowly and with a good amount of effort, but once he was firmly situated upon his feet, he gave a humble bow towards the crowd in front of him, and they cheered again. A hollered “hooray” was heard when he’d scooped up the woman who came to stand at his side —his wife, Eliza— into his arms and charmed her with a kiss. She smiled against his cheek and mouthed something that Lena couldn’t quite make out, but the cheering slowly dwindled as everyone inside moved in to give him hearty pats on the back, and sat each of their hats upon his head. Boys, the lot of them, yet graying in their gruff middle age.

    Lena remarked inwardly that Jeremiah was quite handsome and that he possessed that unique southern charm which most men had here, mostly in the way that they were proud to dote upon the women in their lives. Their mothers, wives, daughters, and nieces… He chatted with Lena from his chair about his girls for a while, about the “no good” they got up to as a pair on occasion and how he was more proud of them than anything else on earth. Lena shared her sentiments that she was glad to have Kara working there at the vineyard and offered him a tour for whenever he’d like. He seemed quite keen to take her up on it, she thought.

    After a while, Nia had found her way up onto a bar stool and gave a shrill whistle towards the crowd in order to gather their attention. “Alright, alright! Thank you all for coming!” She said. The way she could command a room was impressive. Lena liked that about her, as well as her many other wonderful qualities. “I’ll make this quick because we’ve got a cake to cut- but I wanted to take a moment to express our gratitude for this wonderful space here at Shade Tree Cellars. To Lena Luthor and Jack Spheer, thank you for giving us the bar!”

    Another roar went out inside the restaurant with more sounds of clapping, and Lena tucked her chin with a quick nod from where she leaned against a far window. “We’ll be needing it back tomorrow, make no mistake.” She shouted back.

    A laugh rang out and Nia continued, “That reminds me, for the wine drinkers- there’s a limited number of cases set aside for purchase, so be sure to at least grab a bottle on your way out. You won’t be disappointed!” Then she gave a great -clap- of her hands together to bring her speech to a close, “Lastly, to Jeremiah…”

    A hush fell over the crowd, but the man in the wheelchair gave a brisk wave of his hand, “Still here, don’t worry.”

    Another laugh. Nia’s face turned from its excitable demeanor to something a bit softer then, as if her heart was so swollen and full of love for the man that she was near to bursting with it. “We love you so much. Happy birthday, dad.”

    The kitchen doors came swinging open right about then, and out from behind the bar stepped Kara and Alex both as they carried a cake. It had so many candles stuck into it that their collective flame gathered in its middle, likely a joke upon the man’s age. All in good fun, Lena assumed. Subtle teasing was a love language in these parts, she had come to learn. The cheering after that didn’t stop for a good long while, but then everything once again faded to a dull roar as the many inside the restaurant resumed their animated and engaging conversations with one another.

    These were hardworking tradespeople, Lena surmised. Truckers, and fieldhands, and stonemasons, and lumber workers, all of them having arrived from both near and far for one single man. Lena had never seen a more beloved display for celebrating one person’s birthday. Things really were so vastly different here… Compared to living anywhere else, that is. The raucous noise of it all eventually brought her outside to face the courtyard, and from there her feet began to wander towards the tall privet hedges just beyond. Jack had at least shown up for the occasion and had quickly taken to talking about his wine (or himself, rather) as soon as the opportunity presented itself. They hadn’t spoken since the night she’d broken off their engagement. It seemed that he had gotten the message; To unstick his nose from where it didn’t belong. Lena felt as though she’d grieved enough over their friendship long enough, but she didn’t wish to have him feeling spurned forever. She just wanted her friend back.

    For now, it was nice just to have one less thing to worry about.

    Somehow, Lena ended up at her private corner hedge spot with her patio chair and matching table. Her body knew the number of turns inside the maze by heart, so it wasn’t any wonder that she’d ended up there, really. It was just another place she knew of to seek out solace. The weather was also fair enough for a brief respite. The fresh air was nice. Lena pulled in a deep breath to take it all in.

    The end of summer was quickly approaching, and much to her joy, they were ahead of schedule with the last of their second-year harvest. Having such a gap before cultivating their third would leave time enough for the administration side of things, and for investing in some much needed renovations to the villa. That is, of course, if they planned on staying… which Lena very much did. The afternoon was beautiful, and Lena was content just to bathe in her happiness.

    The soft -thud- of wooden bootheel upon mulch turned Lena out of her solitude in time to witness someone emerging from behind a trimmed hedge corner. She smiled at the sight of them; The brilliant sun walked upon the earth in front of her, and its name was Kara.

    “I, um,” Kara wrung her hands together quietly, “I hope it’s alright if I join you?”

    “Of course.” Lena nodded, feeling her cheek dimple for how badly it wished to break out into a smile.

    Kara huffed a laugh once she came up alongside the small table. “So many farmers in one place… Gets a little loud whenever they get something to drink in ‘em.”

    “I noticed.” Lena teased. “I will say, though, I find it endearing that everyone calls your father ‘dad’. It’s obvious that he’s cherished by many people.”

    “He takes in a lot of strays, that’s for sure.” Kara smiled. “Me, and Nia, and—”

    “You?” Lena asked with a furrowed brow. The last thing she had expected was for Kara to not be Jeremiah’s biological daughter. They seemed so alike, but then again, the argument of nature versus nurture was withstanding for a reason. It was still a bit staggering to know the truth, at any rate. Kara had Jeremiah’s kind eyes, as well as his strong chin, and his charm… Oh, did she ever have his charm.

    Kara shrugged a little. “Alex is a few years older than me, but she’s theirs. They took me in when I was a kid, and the rest is history. Nia came along much later when we were in high school. Things were a lot rockier for her at first. People could be… unspeakably cruel in the face of things they don’t want to understand.”

    “What, because she’s trans?”

    Kara reared her head back at that, almost instinctively. “Wow, you just come right out and say it, don’t you?”

    “It’s not like it’s a bad word or anything…”

    “No, I mean you’re right. I guess I’m just not used to someone being so, I don’t know, blasé about it. Not around here, at least.” Kara shrugged again, “To me, she’s just Nia.”

    “Agreed.” Lena smiled, “Your dad seems to be feeling quite well.”

    “Eh, he’s got his good days and bad days. Everyone really pulled together whenever his cancer was diagnosed. It was pretty scary for a while at first, but things are lookin’ up.”

    “I’m glad to hear it. I can tell that you love your family very much.” Lena sighed, “It’s really nice to witness.”

    Kara paused for a moment with that, shifting her boots idly into the faded red mulch with a thought. “I’m guessing you don’t have the same experience?”

    “A loving family? Hm.” Lena thumbed at one of the unfastened buttons of her blazer and drew it back with her hand upon her hip. “They love me in their own way, I suppose. My stepmother and father, and my half-brother. It’s the ‘never being good enough’ sort of struggle that I face. Not my favorite topic of conversation, honestly.”

    “I understand.”

    Comfortable silence was something that came and went with a sense of ease between them. It was almost natural, long after the overwhelming urge just to fill it with more words had passed. Lena no longer felt like she needed to drop anchor with more conversation just to keep Kara interested in her mere presence. No, Kara had made it pretty clear by now. She not only didn’t mind Lena’s company, but she tended to enjoy it. There was just one thing that Lena desperately wished to get off her chest…

    “I’ve noticed for a while now that you leave sometimes late at night- only because I’m out walking, and I see you drive away. I’m not like, watching you or anything…”

    That last part tasted like a lie, but it felt worth mentioning so that Kara wouldn’t get the wrong idea. But then again, what idea really was there to get? God, Lena just wanted to tell her.

    I broke it off with Jack.

    And I think about you constantly.

    I even dream about you sometimes.

    I hate that I’m your boss…

    I wish things were different.

    “Do you…” Lena swallowed, suddenly nervous now, “I don’t know, do you have somebody special that you—”

    “No.” Kara replied quickly.

    “Oh.”

    “It’s nothing like that.”

    Kara seemed to ball her fists quite a lot. A habit, perhaps, maybe from soreness more than anything. Sometimes whenever she did so, her knuckles gave a few deep -pop- sounds for how tightly she opened and closed them in repetition. Lena noticed that Kara only did such a thing whenever she was contemplating a problem and was deep in the throes of searching out a solution. Here inside the hedged maze of the villa’s courtyard, Kara furled one of her hands into a fist.

    Lena’s eyes shot there at once, feeling the action to be just a bit out of place as per their more normal interactions. She noticed straight away the bluish bruises which sat every so often there upon her knuckles. If Kara wore padded gloves when she worked out with her heavy bag, then why were the backs of her knuckles always so beaten and bruised? The concerned feelings that lived behind Lena’s ribs wished more than anything to break free of their bonds, and Lena finally reached to take Kara’s hand into both of her own. Again, that natural, tactile relationship they had forged over the summer came into its own again. Kara made no effort to take her hand back, and Lena turned it towards the amber glow of that late afternoon just to smooth her thumbs back and forth there. She thought about the bruise that had once lived at the back of Kara’s jaw, as well as the few days’ worth of dark circles that had appeared beneath her eyes. Lena wanted to ask… The question was there, ready and waiting upon the tip of her tongue…

    What on earth do you do to yourself?

    As it turned out, the only words that seemed less scary to her at the time were the ones that came out in the form of a confession, rather than a question.

    “I’m sorry that I kissed you.”

    Much to her surprise, Kara answered quickly once again. “I’m not.”

    Lena’s breath went out from her chest in one fell swoop.

    There it was again. The absence of air, and words, and all forms of thought. All of a sudden Kara stepped forward, and on instinct, Lena took a matching step back. Why did her knees feel only one split-second away from collapse? That incessant, flapping feeling deep within her gut had made a reappearance. Kara seemed to hold herself back from taking another step for a moment, but when she did follow through, Lena’s feet backpedaled once again. She didn’t know why. The rest, though, happened very quickly.

    Kara pushed forward again, and suddenly Lena found herself flat against the tall hedge that stood at her back. The sharp points of its waxy leaves poked at the backs of her arms through her blazer, forcing her to take in a surprised gasp through the thin space of her teeth. She had blinked and Kara was in front of her. Tall and beautiful, and warm like spring sunshine. She was so close that Lena could give one bat of her lashes and have them brush right up against Kara’s cheek. The breaths that fell heavier now from Kara’s nose filled the sliver of space between them. Lena felt as if she stood upon a precipice; toes hanging just over the edge of a cliff and mustering the courage to jump—

    “I just don’t want to hurt anyone.” Kara said.

    Lena’s reply fell breathy and impatient upon Kara’s lips. “You haven’t. You won’t.”

    Damn her hands for the way they wanted to climb upward. To lay against this woman at her broad shoulders and to pull… Lena could feel Kara’s eyes burning a path from her eyes to her lips, to her quivering chin…

    …and then Kara took a step back.

    Lena felt as though another hole had been torn right through her middle. The visceral sort of loss was almost too much to bear— She felt like begging.

    But before she could open her mouth, she watched Kara’s as it went to open and shut in search of something to say. Despite her apparent desire, it seemed that there was nothing left to be said. Except for everything.

    Kara’s reddened cheeks grew buoyant once again whenever she forced a smile beneath them, and then she turned around and simply left. Lena’s hand went to the thundering of her own heart, pressing down there just to keep the damn fool thing inside of her chest.

    “Oh, my God.”

    Risks, All

    Chapter Summary

    Lena deals with secrets, both kept and exposed.

    Chapter Notes

    CW: violence

    Chapter Six: Risks, All

     

    “Absolutely. Sure. Mhm. No- Thank you.”

    Lena went to sit back down upon the hard wood surface of her spindle back chair and then turned to speak over her shoulder, “That’s another case sold.” She huffed a laugh in her chest, stuffing her phone back into her slacks, “Jeremiah’s party really got the ball rolling. We can hardly label fast enough.”

    “I’ll say…” Nia gave a -thwip- of her thumb where a long wine bottle label was stuck. Several others seemed to also be glued to the legs of her bellbottom jeans and flowing top— She was simply covered in Shade Tree Pinot Noir labels. One had even been stuck to the elegant point of her chin, and she took it back with a quick and quiet “ouch”.

    Labeling was, admittedly, Lena’s least favorite part of manufacturing saleable wine… Apart from bottling, of course. There was always too much noise involved with bottling, what with all of the rollers and conveyors and biting -clink- noise they made against so much of their recycled glass. It just had a way of setting her teeth on edge. Jack had been the one to stand around and supervise the bottling process with a wide, almost prideful grin smacked squarely upon his face. Dollar signs practically grew from his eyes in a whimsical sort of tweety bird-like fashion. At least he was done moping around everywhere, though he’d already begun to dote upon Lena with small gestures in order to regain her favor. He left her flowers and gifts, and he even lingered around her quiet places sometimes in the hopes that he might catch her, and they might talk.

    “I’m just glad it all turned out alright.” Lena went on, “I’ll admit, I had my doubts there for a while.”

    Nia chuckled beneath her breath as she thumbed another label smooth onto a bottle, “Everybody’s been talking enough about it- I’m not surprised they want more. With a cab as good as that, who knows, you could probably even afford to automate all these labels someday.”

    “Wouldn’t that be nice—” Lena stopped, “Wait. Did you say the cabernet?”

    Nia pulled her lips into her mouth quickly as if she’d misspoken.

    “Nia.”

    “Hm?”

    “We didn’t sell the cabernet.”

    “Mm.”

    “…Did we?”

    Nia finally billowed from the shoulders down, like it was all she could stand just to keep it secret and to herself. “I just wanted to help.”

    “Wait, wait, wait. Are you saying…” Lena flustered, heart rate spiked and near to the roof now, “Are you saying that we’ve been selling the cabernet this entire time?”

    Nia went to bob her head back and forth undecidedly. “Well—”

    “Nia.”

    “Technically we sold the last of it at the party, so, what’s going out now is the pinot.” She flapped a sticky label back and forth from the end of one of her fingers, “So… no worries!”

    Lena stood up quickly from her chair and ran to the pallets stationed near the loading doors of the winery. Straw filled crates were stacked upon every one of them, and each bottle Lena tore after only confirmed her fears… This was where the pallet of cabernet had sat, except now there were newly labeled bottles of pinot noir in their place. “These are not actually the cabernet…?”

    Nia shook her head no.

    Lena felt dizzy all of a sudden. It’s not like her cabernet was actually vinegar, or that she hadn’t actually intended to sell it, she just hadn’t expected it to be sold so quickly. She turned around on the heels of her comfortable flats and then placed her hands upon her hips, trying her absolute best to remain calm. “No… Because we’ve sold the cabernet…”

    “Mhm.” Nia nodded nervously.

    “Do you know what this means?”

    “Um."

    Lena threw her hands into the air to emphasize her dread, “It means that when people realize they’re drinking a pinot instead of a cabernet, they’re going to feel that they’ve been lied to.”

    “Oh,” Nia waved a reassuring hand at her then, “I wouldn’t worry about that. To these folks, red wine is red wine.”

    “There’s an entirely different flavor profile! Different grapes!”

    “I promise, it’ll be fine—”

    “Nia!”

    No More Secrets

    Chapter Summary

    Lena and Kara take a trip.

    Chapter Notes

    Chapter Seven: No More Secrets

     

    It was true that Lena spent a lot of time in her room.

    When she wasn’t working or walking the grounds of her beloved vineyard, she was knees to chest under the soft, heavy duvet of her bed, or had her head hung low in her shower in the hopes that everything that made her feel so filthy and undeserving would simply wash down the drain.     Fixed her makeup.

I only want to tell you that I’m going to do everything I can to remind you of the young man you befriended back in college. The one who didn’t have any friends, and who spilled a container full of fruit fly cultures on your sun dress, and who you threw a microscope at—”

    “Jack…” Lena felt her shoulders droop heavily, “I know that’s who you are. You’ll always be my greatest friend… You just got yourself a little lost, and I just- I want things to go back to the way they were.”

    “I should have never gone along with your parent’s idea of marriage. I’m sorry I made you feel like you didn’t have any choice.”

    “It’s okay.”

    “I love you, Lena.”

    “I love you too.”

    “I just don’t wanna marry you…”

    Lena laughed finally. It felt like such a relief; Jack used to make her laugh so often. “I don’t wanna marry you either.”

    “Good. Then we’re agreed. I’m on your side, do you hear me? I’ll make you proud of me again, I promise.”

    “Alright.”

    “Now get out there and blow these distributor bigwig’s tastebuds to kingdom come.”

    “We want them to buy our wine, Jack, not have the top of their heads come flying off because of it.”

    “Not our wine…” Jack said warmly, “Yours.”

    Chapter End Notes

    I had a pretty good interview today for a new job and I think they may want me to start as early as Monday, but we'll see.

    Sunshine and Prickly Pears

    Chapter Summary

    Lena visits the Danvers family farm.

    Chapter Notes

    Chapter Eight: Sunshine and Prickly Pears

     

    Fall in the hill country was timely, if not surprisingly chilly.

    The weeks of breaking summer heat went in a flash. Rolling hills, barren valleys and short tiered trees made for brisk and unforgiving winds, but while the sun was still out each day, Marble Falls did indeed carry on an “autumn for the ages” sort of vibe. The maple trees were quickly fading to their sugary reds and yellows. They dropped their brittle detritus of leaves and loose bark upon the ground as if to shed and grow dormant for the coming harsh season of cold. Always prepared, the Box Elder and Bigtooth.

    Makeshift tables and the bannered cloths that topped them were weighted down with pumpkins everywhere Lena looked. Some carved, some painted… They seemed to accent every single booth that she walked past.

    The annual grape festival was in full swing.

    Nia had one-half of her face painted with a butterfly’s wing. She’d thought it was the most wonderful thing— Lena agreed as it only complemented her light-hearted nature. Nia was full of whimsy and wonder, and the fact that everything, no matter how small or insignificant, appeared beautiful to her. She could find it anywhere, beauty. A rare skill, or a perfectly “Nia” trait… Regardless, Lena wished she could be more like Nia on most days. She smiled at the sight of Brainy lifting a spoonful of what looked like peach cobbler to Nia’s lips. He was a man of few words, yes, but his eyes said everything. How he cherished her…

    Some of the tablecloths flapped in the wind as Lena continued her slow ambling down this aisle of hand-made jewelry and the next. The wives of farmers were certainly an artsy bunch whenever they weren’t rearing their children. Banjo music played upon the breeze while Lena walked further towards the open pumpkin patch near the end of a stage. A flatbed truck for hayrides was preparing to leave— children of all ages climbed on top and elbowed playfully with one another for the best seats. An idea sparked suddenly at the back of Lena’s mind to make use of her vineyard’s large hedge maze… Perhaps something for Halloween? The holiday would be upon them quickly enough. She would have to remember to run it past Nia, for sure.

    Everyone had their hands full with festive foods and shopping bags, as well as children too small to be left to themselves. They all knew each other by name, some even going back generations. Lena listened here and there amongst them while they spoke idly with one another —with half the things they said going right over her head, of course— but it all filtered down yet again to the same good-natured conclusion: They all cared for one another. Despite tales of generational quarrels, misplaced cattle, and disaster from one year to the next, everyone seemed to get along. Or at least they pretended to for the sake of social etiquette. It was hard to tell, sometimes. But they all loved the dirt they were raised up on, that much was clear to see. The land they tended put food on their tables, as well as shoes on their children’s feet.

    The sound of cheering rose over the stacked straw bales and brought Lena’s attention towards the wide end of the stage again. There at the back of it sat two barrel targets.

    A small crowd of people pushed their way to the front, urging two individuals up onto the elevated stage with loud hoorays and words of encouragement. Lena crossed her arms and watched on.

    It was Kara and her sister Alex, bowing and laughing along with the rest of the people down below. For a moment, Lena couldn’t be sure— some of the men who stood before the stage seemed to look very much like the same ones who had been present that night at the fight club. They had cheered then just as they cheered now. It turned a sour feeling in the pit of Lena’s stomach just at the thought. She couldn’t understand it. Maybe Kara would have a better time explaining if Lena could only gather the gumption to ask.  

    The Danvers sisters seemed to drawl openly at something left upon the stage for them, rolling their eyes both in an altogether unsurprised fashion. Alex went to pick something up off a small fold-out table there, which glinted with a shiny, almost metallic quality in the crisp fall sunlight.

    Throwing knives, Lena realized.

    After carefully handing a handful of them to Kara, Alex gave an unsure wave back at the rowdy crowd, egging them on in good spirit. They shouted at once back at her. It seemed they were eager for a show. More onlookers from nearby began to flock to the stage like it was an expected event. Maybe they do this every year? Lena wondered. She was sore with herself for a split-second, wishing that she’d only left the security of her nice, cozy villa to come and see the local festivities for herself the year before.

    Kara and Alex locked eyes suddenly, practiced and patient in their staring right down to the slow steps that drew them away from one another. The crowd fell with a hush, and then it was so quiet that Lena could hear the -creak- of the long boards upon the stage.

    In the blink of an eye, a knife each came flying out from the sister’s hands and towards the barrels. They landed deeply there at their red painted centers, and the people down below gave out a loud, crashing cheer. Lena was ecstatic— Kara’s smile was so beautiful. Her proud and rosy cheeks made the palms of Lena’s hands itch for how badly they wanted to alight there. To take her up by her stunning jaw, and to breathe in the feeling of her full, pink lips…

    The sisters seemed to share a few words mixed with happy laughter as the crowd heckled them some more.

    Alex stepped forward again.

    Lena could see firsthand now how much Kara admired her older sister. She looked on at her with a steep sense of awe, eyes bright and glistening in the sun. The way the voices shouted from below were a bit more familiar for Alex— as if she were the one to entertain long before the act had become a duo. Alex rounded out her shoulder from her short sleeve shirt and shifted her boots with a prepared stance, and then a count of seven knives were thrown from her hand, calmly and precisely, though, none of them hit her barreled target at the back of the stage.

    The crowd seemed to scratch their heads for a moment until Alex motioned towards them with a finger, asking them to wait.

    She hopped down off the stage past the pair of targets and ran quite a bit further towards a tall stack of straw bales. From there she returned with a pumpkin. She climbed back upon the stage a few moments later with an eager hop, and then turned the large pumpkin in her hands around to face the people down below. The shape of a slightly crooked smile was struck there in its orange flesh with all seven knives.  

    Everyone burst into a proud holler all at once.

    Lena was beyond impressed. Perhaps that was a defining trait of all Danvers women, to be wholly impressive and jaw-dropping, but to also remain impeccably humble. Getting to know Alex better in the near future was certainly something Lena was looking forward to… She would just have to make more time to actually talk with Kara, first. They’d been doing a lot together lately, but truthfully, very little of it consisted of talking. Lena shivered at the thought of Kara’s hands at her hips, and her mind began to wander…

    Somebody in the crowd shouted out Kara’s name all of a sudden, and then a flash of red came up high over their head. Kara spun and threw a knife from her hip, landing it with a solid -thock- sound against the object thrown into the air.

    Lena’s jaw did very much drop that time.

    …It was an apple.

    One more went up into the cloudless blue sky at the other end of the crowd, and then another at its furthest reach. Kara claimed both in quick succession.

    -thock-

    -thock-

    Clapping soon carried over the sounds of laughter and all-around-excitement. Satisfied (or merely out of knives) Kara and Alex waved and then were helped down from the stage with proud pats upon their backs and hugs around their necks. Beyond the shadow of a doubt, Lena realized, the Danvers sisters were the darlings of Marble Falls.

    As soon as the crowd dispersed back to their jaunty walks with family and friends, Kara’s sunny disposition looked right in Lena’s direction and, upon catching sight of her, went to shoulder her way through one small group of bystanders after another in order to join the quiet, empty booth end where she stood. Lena had leaned there for quite some time while watching Kara and Alex’s knife throwing act, and she scooted to share some of the space for another hip to perch closely to that of her own. Kara looked as though she wanted to close the distance between them on instinct, but remained just this side of respectful so as not to generate suspicion, or rumor. Around here, the latter often went hand in hand with Lena’s very vocation; word traveled fast through the grapevine.

    Somewhere in the distance there was a voice over a bullhorn announcing a grape weighing contest, and the people standing around seemed to dwindle in that direction more and more. Kara was seemingly uninterested; Her magnificent blue eyes stayed trained on Lena’s lips. She was insatiable. For this, Lena was pleased beyond words.

    “So?” Kara asked.

    So, I think I’m in love with you…

    Lena swallowed hard. “Knives, huh?”

    Kara gave a shrug. “Alex taught me.”

    “I gathered.” Lena laughed. She crossed her arms over her chest a moment later, scared that her hands might furl themselves into Kara’s downy soft turtleneck sweater. She couldn’t help it, but she thought more and more about the people in the crowd who had seemed just as pleased to see Kara previously hurt as they were to see her throw knives. It grated at the back of her mind until the words simply came out, “Can I ask you about something?”

    Kara nodded as she took a long arm to hang above them at the booth’s rickety top. An apt stance, as she was rather monkey-like in her curious nature just as she was her physical nature. She was always hanging on things. It made her arms look incredible. “Sure.” She said.

    “I recognized some of the people in the crowd—”

    Kara’s eyes swung low to her boots then, and her arm came back down. She knew exactly what Lena was referring to. Once again, she seemed ashamed. “…Yeah.”

    “How,” Lena flustered for a moment, “I don’t know how they can just pretend like nothing ever happened.”

    “Because we agree to.” Kara said plainly, “Because they’re too guilty, and I’m too guilty, and no one wants to fess up and admit that I got my bell good and rung.”

    “Why, though? I understand the guilt, believe me, I’ve got my own, but I couldn’t bear to watch you go through that more than once… How do you just go along with it all?”

    “There’s—” Kara sighed as she dusted her hands a bit, “There’s no good way to explain it, I guess. It’s just the way things are. If there’s one thing these folks understand more than anything, it’s a sense of duty. They knew why I was there, sure enough, we don’t exactly get in each other’s way when it comes to family. I’m obligated to take care of my father.”

    Lena shook her head with a long breath buffeted through her lips, “I’m trying my best here, but I just… I don’t know if I’ll ever be able to look at anyone the same, knowing everything.” Then another thought occurred to her, “Your sister… Does she know?”

    “Oh, yeah.” Kara nodded, “That day you came to the trailer to give me my job back? That was the day my dad found out- but honestly, it’d been going on for a while before that. He put it together that none of us could come up with that kind of money out of nowhere. He asked me, I told him, Alex and I argued, my mom cried…”

    “I see.”

    “Working at the vineyard gets me away from the disappointed look in his eyes. He doesn’t say it, but I know it. I also knew that I was probably gonna have to keep fighting, so being at home really just felt sort of awful.”

    “I’m sorry you felt like you had to. Fight, I mean.”

    “You know what?” Kara shook her head, “I’m not. My dad’s alive, and he’s getting better. I don’t regret it.”

    She’d said it as though she were trying harder to convince herself rather than anyone else. Lena could only guess at the weight which sat upon Kara’s wonderful shoulders… Did it plague her in ways that Lena couldn’t see? It was awful just to think about, but it was clear by the way Kara continually hung her head. Lena wanted to see it all washed away; To have those same shoulders feather-light and unburdened once more, because oh, what a sight that would truly be. But the fear of Kara continuing to brawl with others for money was a constant source of unease for Lena. The idea of it made her feel nauseous, and most days, it was all she could do just to stuff it back down. Begging Kara to never fight again wasn’t something she could stomach. It felt unfair to even ask. Selfish, even. Kara had flat out refused the money that Lena offered. Additionally, she’d shot down the idea of bonuses, or even a pay increase… At the time, Kara had made it seem like such an exorbitant sum that all of her hard work, no matter how much of it she put in, would never be able to meet.

    No, Lena wouldn’t ask Kara to stop, even though every inch of her heart and soul begged her to. She knew she’d only get the same gut-wrenching answer.

    “Hey—” Kara went to tap at Lena’s elbow, pulling her free from the slippery slope of her anxious spiraling, “Do you wanna see something?”

    Lena finally smiled. “Sure.”

     

     

    The drive to Kara’s family farm had been a short one.

    It was just a little ways down the railroad tracks and across one unpaved road after another. Out here, there were potholes so large that you could snap an axle if you didn’t swerve just in time… Apparently there was a reason that farmers “meander” with their tractors. Treacherous country roads often made graveyards of old cultivating equipment.

    Gnarled tree limbs made crooked fence posts between rows of barbed wire. There was nary a tree in sight beyond their border for all the wide, patchy cactus upon the ground. It stretched for as far as the eye could see. Lena hadn’t been sure what to expect as far as what commercially harvested cacti would look like, but as it turned out, it was just like any other farm in the area; Coordinated, equipped, and sitting upon more land than one typically knew what to do with.

    Things were just a bit overgrown, it seemed. Lena quickly assumed that there wasn’t exactly anyone taking care of the place, not after the bank had evicted Kara and her family from it. The only home they’d ever known was a ghost town.

    A roof had fallen in on a tool shed nearby and there were gutted pickups on the front lawn, but the rest was still, more or less, a farm by any standard. There were plots with sprawling cacti everywhere, both tall and low to the ground and adorned with many thousands of sharp, piercing spines each. The prickly pears were a bright fuchsia color and had been left to grow in abundance over so much unkempt green cactus. It was sad, really. Generations were spent here —likely farming one crop to the next— just to have it all ripped away and left to succumb to the elements.

    “There’s so much of it…” Lena spun in place once they exited Kara’s bronco. “I never knew there was this much of a demand for commercially grown cactus.”

    “We had a lot of happy people working here for a long time.” Kara replied, “And we never went hungry, that’s for sure.”

    “It’s horrible just to leave it all this way… I know it must hurt to not be able to do anything about it.”

    “It’s alright. I wanted you to see where I grew up.”

    Lena laughed to alleviate some of her guilt. “I’m imagining so many bandaids.”

    Kara shook her head with a smile, “You wouldn’t be far from the truth on that front.” She pointed towards a rusted swing set that sat behind a garage then, “Alex and I played there on those swings more times than I can count- until we were old enough to work, of course.”

    “Just how long have you been working?”

    “I don’t know, I was probably fifteen?” Kara shrugged, “It wasn’t a big deal or anything, I just helped carry stuff and things like that.”

    “So, tell me,” Lena sauntered past Kara to eye a wide cactus patch in all its glory, “What makes a prickly pear so special?”

    Kara took the pair of leather work gloves that she’d tucked beneath her arm and twisted a large purplish fruit from a round, spiny cactus pad. “Lots of things. Wanna try one?”

    “Mhm. Okay.”

    Kara took care to remove what smallish spines she could see and then kneeled down to roll the fruit around in the dirt. “They’ve got lots of invisible thorns on them, so you gotta make sure to get rid of those before you try eating it—” Then she stood back up and jogged to get a water bottle from her truck, “But every part of its edible, actually. The pads, the pears…”

    Lena watched as Kara rinsed the fruit and then took a small pocketknife around it to expose its seedy flesh. With a pinch of her fingers, it came right out. Lena took a good look at it in her own before she popped it into her mouth. Much to her surprise, it tasted almost like watermelon had crossed with a pineapple, and had very small seeds similar to blackberries. “Mm,” She hummed, “That’s actually incredible—” She sucked the juice from the end of her thumb, “You said you can eat the pads too?”

    “Once you get the spines off, yeah. Grilled, blanched, fried- It’s all pretty good.”

    “I’d have never known.” Lena admitted again, amazed.

    “Without my dad running things though, it just… fell apart. We tried, but one thing just led to another, and, well…” Kara trailed off with a sad look in her eye.

    “I’m glad it’s still here at least for you to come visit.”

    “The bank’s pretty lax on keeping it closed up, thankfully. Me and Alex are still trying to find a way to get it all back.”

    Lena stuffed her hands into the pockets of her dark blue peacoat, “I imagine there’s still a market for it, if you had this much…”

    “Mostly local grocers and the odd yuppie who thinks having a cactus patch in their front yard makes them look rugged.”

    Lena laughed at that. “You can’t be serious.”

    “Oh yeah. Landscaping companies were our biggest buyers a lot of the time.” Kara replied with a slightly amused tone. “C’mon,” She went to fish one of Lena’s hands out of her pockets then, “Lemme show you the house.”

    To the small farmhouse’s credit, it was much bigger on the inside than Lena expected. It practically smelled like Kara. Lena could see clearly now why it meant so much to her— other than the fact that she’d lived here for most of her life, of course. There was a story to everything they walked by. Most of the furniture was covered up with drop cloths and plastic sheeting, stored safe and sound since the day they’d left it all behind… Kara regaled her with the notches along the framework of the kitchen, denoting each of the children’s names, heights and ages.

    “Wow, Nia had a hell of a growth spurt— five inches in one year?”

    “Yeah—” Kara laughed, “She shot up like a weed during freshman year. The bullying was, not so great.”

    Lena brushed the side of Kara’s face with a smile, “I’m glad she had you and Alex… Because look at her now- all the better for it.”

    “We did alright.” Kara replied.

    Always so humble…

    “I wish I had met you sooner.” Lena scooted herself between the wall and Kara and went to look up towards those heavenly blue irises, the small galaxies she fell headfirst into time and time again, “Years ago, when I would have needed you most. Everyone deserves to have somebody like you in their lives.”

    Kara seemed to pause with that. “Do I deserve you…?”

    “You’re asking me?”

    “I am.”

    Lena let go of a breath, “I don’t understand. Is there something that makes you think that you don’t?”

    “Just the obvious, I guess.”

    “And what would that be?”

    “That we’re just, so… different. I mean, we come from completely different—”

    “Don’t.” Lena went to cover Kara’s mouth and then moved her lips there with a small kiss. “Please… don’t do that.”

    “It doesn’t bother you at all?”

    “No.” Lena replied with a huff, “What would bother me, though, is knowing that it bothered you.”

    Kara shook her head. “I’m just worried that you’re gonna wake up one day and come to your senses.”

    “About you, you mean?”

    “Yeah.”

    Lena took Kara’s face into her hands again and kissed her several times, wrapping her arms around the back of her neck to hug her all at the same time. “Will you just hold me for a moment, please?” She asked.

    Kara’s arms went completely around Lena’s waist with ease, almost as if they’d been doing so for a lifetime. Lena felt that it only made sense; She’d waited that long and so much more for somebody like Kara. For exactly Kara.

    It killed her to know that Kara didn’t find herself worthy, when really, it was Lena that was sure to think herself lacking in such a substance. She came from a wealthy background and didn’t hardly know the first thing about struggle, so, if anything, Lena was far behind as far as feelings of “worthiness” were concerned. She’d been placed upon her little pedestal for so long and had simply watched the world go by. Kara anchored her here. Pulled her free from her forlorn emptiness. Filled her up, made her whole…

    And held her like the warm embrace of a radiant sun.

    “You’re the most wonderful person I’ve ever met, Kara.” Lena leaned her head back to rest against the wall, “I adore you. You have a heart that’s so, so big- and I’m so drawn to you because of it. Not because I’m fascinated by you- which I am- but I want to be near you always because you’re the part of me that I’ve always wanted, but have never had the courage to find on my own. You make me want to become who I’m meant to be. I only feel selfish for it… I feel like all I do is take.”

    “That’s fine, because all I’ve ever wanted to do is give.” Kara tucked her chin into Lena’s neck then with a long sigh. She squeezed her again. It felt so good.

    Lena couldn’t believe how wonderful it was just to stand there and be still with Kara. Long, deep breaths went out from her nose, wafting with a warm sense of belonging down inside the stiff lapels of her coat and over her chest. Kara had indeed breathed life back into her.

    You’re everything…

    Kara eventually pulled herself from the warm confines of Lena’s neck and blinked happily at her for a moment, eyes smiling just like they always did. “Ready to head back?” She asked.

    Lena nodded. “Okay.”

    On their way out, Lena watched as Kara doubled back for a split second to don her gloves and twist a small handful of prickly pears from the tops of a particularly wide cactus patch. She then climbed into the front seat of her truck and made a shrugged sort of “what” sound towards Lena as she rubbed the pears around inside the leather of her gloves, perhaps to brush their sharp invisible hairs away.

    “So, mine gets rolled around in the dirt but yours don’t?”

    Kara chuckled. “I don’t mind tiny little thorns in my mouth.”

    “Well,” Lena huffed a laugh at that, “If you’re to ever kiss me again, I’ll thank you to have a thorn-free mouth.”

    Kara came to an almost comical stop, freezing there with her hands full of cactus fruit. Then she rolled her window down and tossed them right out. Lena laughed out loud.

    “Oh, my goodness.” She teased. “I thought you liked them?”

    “Eh. Kissing you’s way better.”

    Lena tilted her chin up proudly once Kara turned the key into the ignition. The large engine block came to life with a roar. “Good answer.”

    “Technically, we’re not supposed to take anything else away, but I come by and grab a few pails full every once in a while.”

    “Oh? What do you do with them?”

    “I just snack on ‘em, mostly. Alex likes to strain them into margaritas. They make pretty good wine, too.”

    “Oh, now you’re talking. A margarita sounds—” Lena stopped, “Wait. Wine?” Her eyebrows went up with a stiff raise, “Prickly pear wine?”

    “Yup.”

    “…Kara.”

    “Hm?”

    “I think I know how to get your farm back.”

    Chapter End Notes

    Ended up getting the job. Expect updates to go back to normal with a handful of days in between. These two... *sigh* so, so soft. I love them.

    Thanks for reading <3

    Freedom Is a Cold Wind

    Chapter Summary

    Lena visits Sam in New York.

    Chapter Notes

    The additional tags have been updated.

    Chapter Nine: Freedom Is a Cold Wind

     

    “Stairs…” Lena said with a tired huff, “I’m remembering now how much I hated your stairs.”

    Sam laughed as she ran a key into the front door of her New York apartment and shouldered her way inside. “Missing your very flat digs back in Texas already?”

    Lena thought immediately of Kara and shivered. Sam eyed her with a hint of suspicion while she went to step past her, so she merely brushed it off as the cold. Such a brief stint with reality had set the frigid New York air upon her skin, shocking her back to her senses. Her head had been such a traitorous amalgam of lip-biting memories that day— Ever since she’d stepped off the plane at JFK, Kara filled every available corner of her mind. Naught but a spare thought was left untouched by her blonde goddess of a foreman. The cold of early snow here only made her want to press the end of her freezing nose into the warm space of Kara’s neck, so remembering her kept her warm enough. It was Kara’s hands around the tops of her thighs on their way to the restaurant that evening, and the feeling of Kara’s grunted breath against the side of her neck while Lena rode her like a proud panhandle wind on their way back…

    “Hey—” Sam’s voice brought her ripping back to the present once again, “You’ve been sort of strange all day. Care to tell me what’s bothering you?”

    Lena couldn’t help the laugh that went out from her chest. A second later she was calm and collected once again, doing her best to school the features of her face lest she give away the insane amount of sex she’d been having. That would be a very loud and excited conversation should it happen any time soon. She knew Sam. And besides that, her trip all the way from Texas had sapped most of her energy for that day, leaving her feeling quite dead on her feet. Later… Lena thought as she slid out from her long, suede jacket and took the scarf from her around her neck in long loops.

    “Sorry.” She apologized, “There’s just some things developing at the vineyard- I’m still working it all out, of course, but it looks promising.”

    “Like?” Sam flopped herself down at the end of her couch with a sigh and then busied her hands with taking the long, snip toe boots from each of her feet. A strict eye for fashion, this one. Lena often missed their comparing outfits and exorbitant shopping sprees.

    Lena went to sit at the other end of the couch and did the same. “We’re working on a new wine.” She confessed as she pulled her legs beneath herself and took a small, fuzzy throw pillow to hug against her chest. “Or, we will be.”

    Sam was intrigued. “Really?”

    “It’s nowhere near production scale right now. There’s acquiring the land, for one thing, and I’m still hashing out all the details with—”

    “What do you mean? Don’t you already have plenty of grapes?”

    Lena shook her head, biting the insides of her cheeks just to keep her smile at bay. “It’s not made from grapes.”

    “Then what the hell else do you make—”

    “Prickly pear.”

    Sam blinked, leaving her jaw to hang just ever so slightly. “I’m almost scared to ask. What’s a prickly pear?”

    Lena took her eyes to the empty fireplace and gestured there with a hand, wanting it lit to at least warm her legs, “May I?”

    “Yeah- please, it’s freezing.”

    Lena stacked a few split log pieces and then closed the grate over the hearth once she lit a leftover starter. “It’s amazing how you have one of the only apartments in the city that still has a fireplace.”

    “Right- amazing- lucky- whatever—” Sam waved her off, apparently still very intent on learning more. “You were talking about prickly pears.”

    It seemed that Lena’s subtle segue had led to nowhere. She wasn’t sure why she was so hesitant to bring up Kara… Perhaps it was because once she did, she was afraid she might not be able to stop. But where was the harm in telling Sam, exactly? Maybe she should just come clean. Lena trusted Sam with everything. She always had.

    “Right. Um, it grows on cactus.”

    “Oh- those little red fruits?” Sam quirked her chin, “Are they any good?”
