    “Can I say something crazy?” Lena asked.

    “Hm?”

    “I think, maybe, people are supposed to hurt each other? To a certain extent, anyway. I don’t think this,” Lena gestured between them, “Is supposed to be easy- by any means. I think it’s supposed to be just as uncomfortable as it is wonderful… if that makes sense? It’s that little gap where hurt feelings are supposed to heal, and where we’re supposed to learn something from it, but lately it just feels like we’re stuck there. Aren’t we?”

    “Maybe.”

    “So, how are we even supposed to know if you just keep shutting me out?”

    “I—” Kara clenched her jaw out of habit. The dull -click- sound it made all the way from the back interrupted the gentle rain, just long enough for Kara to rethink her current position. “I just need more time. Is that okay? I’ll work my way up to it.”

    Lena sighed when she dropped her forehead against Kara’s. She tucked a few strands of her vibrant blonde hair behind one of her ears and then nodded there. “You promise?”

    “I promise.”

    “We have to talk more, Kara.” Lena said with another deep breath.

    That would be the understatement of the year… seeing as how they’d done so very little of it even before their separation. Knowing now that deeper conversation was a must, Lena was content just to draw a hard line.

    “I know.” Kara replied.

    “Is there something I can do to help?”

    Kara shrugged, “Just wait for me? I hate to ask, but—” 

    Lena stopped her with a kiss, both hands wrapped around her face and pulling her away from the seat. A long-held breath went out and then Lena pressed herself flush against Kara so as to hug her as tight as she could. “I’ll be right here.” She said, “Forever, if that’s how long it takes.”

    “Alright.”

     

     

    Playful squabbling was a common occurrence in the Danvers household.

    There was still furniture being moved around, so that meant there were at least five different “correct” ways to do so. Nia and Kara could be heard all the way from the back bedroom of the old farmhouse, arguing about which way to leave a sewing machine table. The old timey Singer 99 came mounted atop a wooden desk, and the skinny legs it stood upon gave out a chill-sending screech each time it was yanked back and forth across the bedroom floor. Nia thought it should face the window. Kara thought the window caught too much sun and would cook their mother before she could so much as stitch a nightdress.

    Lena chuckled quietly to herself in the kitchen, listening to the sisters squawk animatedly at one another to no avail. She was glad to be back at Danvers Farm, and even more so to have its original occupants living there in it once again.

    “Smart money’s on Nia.” Alex said where she stood comfortably next to Lena. They were skinning and chopping vegetables for a Mother’s Day dinner, and Lena was more than thrilled just to be included.

    “Oh yeah?”

    “Yeah. She can wear down a bobcat and still end up getting her way.”

    “I believe it.” Lena smiled, “Hey, thanks for inviting me, by the way- I’m really happy to be here.”

    “Kara insisted.” Alex replied with a shrug, not even bothering to look back up from her carrot board.

    Lena still wasn’t quite sure what to make of Alex. Even after all this time, every other thing that came out of her mouth either sounded like “I hate you” or “You might not be so bad after all”. Perhaps she was trying to figure Lena out as well… That would make the most sense, anyway.

    “And besides,” She went on, “…our mom would be happy to see you too- so, there’s that.”

    “Can I ask you something?”

    Alex puffed out her cheeks with a long sigh, almost as if she thought she’d walked right into a trap. “Sure.”

    “Why don’t you like me?”

    Alex’s knife landed with a quiet -clatter- upon the cutting board in front of her, and then she turned towards Lena to fold her arms across her chest… More evidence that she possessed little in the way of patience. “I don’t not like you.” She confessed.

    “Okay… but why does that feel like a non-answer?”

    “Maybe because I don’t actually owe you one.”

    “I don’t get it,” Lena placed her own knife down and shook her head, “What did I do?”

    “It’s not anything you did. I mean, besides almost killing my sister with your negligence—”

    “Oh, come on.”

    Alex turned the corners of her mouth down with a frown. “It’s who you are.”

    “What?”

    “You asked. There it is.”

    “I don’t understand…”

    “What do you want me to say? That you walk around this place acting like you’re better than everything- like some kinds of hard work are just beneath you, or something?”

    “No, because that would be the furthest thing from the truth.” Lena replied, aghast at the accusation.

    Alex narrowed her eyes and shook her head again. “I just don’t see what she sees in you. Sure, you might be a nice person by some New York standards,” She continued, “…but Kara’s a bit naïve whenever it comes to matters of the heart.”

    “No, she’s not—”

    “She’s got our mom completely over the moon with you, being the first and all.”

    You don’t know anything at all. Kara loves me—

    “Wait- what?” Lena felt her shoulders fall a bit slack with that, “What do you mean ‘first’?”

    Alex gave an almost disbelieving grin, squirreling her reddish eyebrows together with a sense of incredulity. “Just that you’re—” Then she stopped, “She hasn’t told you, has she?”

    “Told me what?”

    Alex nearly laughed. “Of course she hasn’t.”

    Lena slid further down the kitchen counter to speak a bit more privately, still listening for the excitable squabbling to die down from the back bedroom. “What am I missing here?”

    “Just what I said… You’re the first.”

    “First what?” Lena demanded.

    “First relationship, as far as I know.” Alex gave a noncommittal shrug and then turned back to her cutting board, “Anything beyond that is not my business.”

    Lena huffed through her nose. Alex’s second statement couldn’t have been any more true— It was none of her business at all that Kara could fuck like an Olympian god. There was no possible way that she’d been a virgin before meeting Lena…

    …but, now at least, the seeds of doubt had been sown.

    “I like you just fine, Lena.” Alex said with a grumble as she took her knife across another carrot with a somewhat hard -chop-, “I just can’t imagine what I’d do if you hurt my sister again.”

    “Stop threatening my boss.” Nia said suddenly from the kitchen doorway. Light on her feet she was, pretty and cunning like that of a fairy. She came in with a skip in her step and took Lena away from the counter, “If it makes you feel better, I can show you where Alex got caught with her first girlfriend—”

    Alex made a wide and rather quick step in front of the laundry room then. “I don’t care how grown you are now… Maggie’s still a low blow.”

    “Yeah? Serves you right—”

    All of a sudden, the front door came creaking open and Alex and Nia both rambled to a stop. Kara came quickly with a hurried stomp all the way to the kitchen, the look upon her face matching that of both her sisters. Lena hid a chuckle in her throat at the sight of them, thinking to herself that the Danvers women truly were a school of their own fish, moving and even thinking as one.

    “They’re back?”

    “No- they’re early—”

    “Crap… go stall!”

    Nia and Kara scurried to get around one another and towards the front foyer, and Alex went to scoop their cut vegetables into a large pot that lay in the sink. Hurrying to clean the countertop of all remaining evidence, she grumbled at the fact that the knife block was all the way at the other end of the kitchen. A flick of her wrist brought the sharp edge of her knife there with a -thock-, just missing its slot at the top. Lena’s jaw dropped at the feat, but her attention was very quickly diverted towards the sounds of shuffling feet at the door. Eliza and Jeremiah were bubbling with questions as to why they couldn’t enter the house, though, they eventually broke through as Eliza appeared to have more shopping bags than she could carry for an extended period of time. Kara helped her to the table, and a lackluster “surprise” went out from all three daughters now standing within the kitchen.

    Eliza crowed with laughter. “For me?” She asked, leaning towards the mouth of the vegetable pot hiding in the sink.

    Mother’s Day dinner would be on early, it seemed.

    “Happy Mother’s Day.” Kara said with a kiss at her temple. Jeremiah gave an approving squeeze at Kara’s shoulder just for the effort, and it made her beam with one of her bright, sunshiney smiles. Lena thought to thank the man; she’d not seen one of those in such a long time.

    “Thank you, honey.” Eliza replied. “Do you want some help—”

    “No!” All three answered in unison once again.

    Eliza surrendered, more astonished than anything. “Alright, alright.” She pecked another kiss upon Kara’s cheek, and then Nia’s, and then Alex’s…

    …And then Lena’s.

    Lena was floored at the gesture. Eliza had greeted her as if she were one of her own, like it took her no effort at all just to be so kind. It filled Lena up with something so warm and loving that she couldn’t quite put it into words— yet it nearly made her want to cry. It felt so right; to be on the receiving end of a proud mother’s kiss. Nothing at all like what Lillian deigned to oblige her with on occasion… This had felt real, and Lena simply adored her for it.

    “I thought the doctor’s office would take all day… What happened?” Kara asked. She stood there tucking the small duck tail of her whimsical print shirt back into the waistband of her jeans and crooked her head in lieu of an answer.

    “Well,” Jeremiah scooted a chair out of the way, “It didn’t take so long this time. We just sat and talked.”

    “…Is that good or bad?” Nia asked.

    “I guess I’d say it was the opposite of bad.”

    Eliza’s hands went up then as a sign of caution once she saw that all three of her daughters had circled around Jeremiah like a shiver of sharks. “Don’t you girls knock him down, now—” 

    “Cancer’s gone, darlin’.”

    Silence swung over the kitchen like a warm wind. It was so quiet that Lena only heard the tail end of a surprised gasp once all three women swarmed their father, each of their arms held out with glee. They fell into him one after the other, knocking him back a single step each time. Jeremiah laughed and buried his face amongst the blonde, auburn, and brown hair of his daughters. Eliza clapped a hand over her mouth to hush her crying, and she eventually made her way over to wrap her arms around them all, completing their small circle.

    “Daddy…” Alex cried. Surprisingly enough, it was her head to turn and look back at Lena with teary eyes. She reached an arm out for her as if to say, “you too”.

    Lena went to her and was suddenly yanked into their fold, squeezing tightly inside all of their happy sniffles and sighs. Kara was sobbing again. Her hands were fisted into Jeremiah’s rumpled collar and pulling there like she’d never been more relieved in her entire life.

    “Everything’s okay now… D’you hear me?” He whispered at Kara’s cheek.

    She nodded and then cried some more.

    Jeremiah eventually hushed and cooed them all enough to let him go, and the air in the kitchen seemed to break with a new sort of atmosphere. He clapped his hands together and then smiled, “Were we cooking, or what?”

    “We need more potatoes!” Nia chimed in, digging the heels of her hands into her eyes to rub the tears from them.

    Lena laughed along with everyone else and then felt someone tug at her hand towards the front door. Kara pulled her along and turned back to speak into the kitchen, grabbing up her keys from the skinny entryway table at the same time. “We’ve got it.” She said, doubling back long enough to kiss at her dad’s cheek just before skipping out through the old foyer with Lena.

    Once they were shut inside of the Bronco, Kara let her hands fall to the tops of her thighs and stared endlessly out through the windshield. A smile was smacked there upon her face still, cheeks both aglow with their happy, reddish tint. There was another tear to streak down from the corner of her eye as Lena reached over to take up one of her hands.

    “Are you alright?” Lena asked.

    Kara turned to look at her and then blinked a few more tears from her baby blues. “More than alright.” She replied before throwing herself across the cab just to plant a kiss at Lena’s lips.

    Lena met her with a happy hum and skirted her fingers along the soft baby hairs of Kara’s nape, looping the end of Kara’s flax colored ponytail around and around while they kissed. She could tell that it felt different, Kara’s kiss… She’d slanted her mouth and was more eager to taste the wet of her tongue, almost like she was suddenly hungrier for Lena.

    Lena was almost dizzy by the time they separated. She raked in a deep breath and fluttered her eyes back open, “What was that for?” She asked.

    Kara huffed a small laugh and kissed the point of her nose as a finishing touch. “I dunno. I just love you.”

    “Are you happy, baby?”

    “Mhm,” Kara nodded. “…so happy.”

    Chapter End Notes

    Wanted to squeeze in a bit more happy before work this week. Usually it's the dad to pretend they don't like somebody... but Jeremiah's just too nice of a guy here. I like prickly-on-the-outside, soft-on-the-inside Alex Danvers. I'm weak, okay?! xD

    Chapter title is a poem fragment by Emily Dickinson.
    Thanks for reading <3

    Words Left Unsaid

    Chapter Summary

    Jack skips town. Nia worries about her relationship with Brainy. Lena makes things clear.

    Chapter Notes

    Chapter Sixteen: Words Left Unsaid

     

    An acrid wind swept between the narrow passages of the hedge maze, breezing a slow sense of unease up into the fine, wispy hairs that lived upon the back of Lena’s neck.

    There was a blood moon in the sky; A lunar eclipse. It dragged its crescent shaped penumbra across the ground in front of her, eking one miniscule inch after another.

    The path ahead seemed like such a yawning gap from here to there. Somewhere in the distance, a parliament of owls gave out their ominous hoots— no doubt to discourage their young branchlings from partaking of the cold, hard ground. That was where the danger lied. Even then (and against her better judgement) Lena felt as if she were walking right into it.

    “Stop…” She said to herself, “Go back.”

    Finding the way back would be difficult, though. She’d not remembered to number her turns. The rolling brume strained through the waxy hedgerow beside her, wafting into a warm pool around her ankles. She could barely see the shredded ends of the mulch beneath it now. It was as if it wished for her to stand still, or at the very least, not to tread any further. Why wouldn’t her feet listen? Hadn’t she only just left her little corner table near the maze’s middle? She realized before long that she was in much deeper than usual; completely at the mercy of nature’s none-too-idle threats.

    Not often did the maze scare her.

    But when it did… She ran.

    Desperate just to find an opening, Lena soon blurred her left from right and stumbled straight ahead, but instead of pricking the palms of her hands upon the small, pointed ends of all the waxy leaves, she’d run right into another warm body.

    A startled shriek went out from her mouth just as she looked up, and then there was Kara, smiling down upon her just as she always did.

    “Woah,” Kara said as she caught Lena with a backwards step, “…slow down.”

    “Oh, my God- you scared me.”

    “Are you lost?”

    “Without you…” Lena huffed a small smile in the place of her anxiety, “Always.”

    Kara’s hand at her cheek helped to soothe the raging stampede of her panic, if only for a moment or two. She swept her knuckles there with a happy glint in her eye and gave a pretty pout with her bottom lip, “I’ve got you now. Don’t worry.”

    As if on cue, the surrounding hedge began to expand, and Lena was finally able to breathe again. The moon was never a more daunting shade of red where it washed against the intense blues of Kara’s eyes, but something about her just standing there seemed so strange… Was there blood running down from her temple? Lena blinked hard and stared at her more closely. No, she decided, just her gorgeous mane spun from gold as it always was, swept carefully over the ends of her ears. Kara herself seemed so ethereal there in the strawberry moon of a full lunar eclipse— almost like she wasn’t even there at all.

    “Am- am I dreaming?”

    “C’mon.” Kara took her by the hand.

    Lena tried wrestling with the fact that this could very well be a dream. That infinitesimally small amount of awareness she could sometimes render during sleep seemed to sift through her fingers like sand the more that Kara pulled at her, and away they went, leaving Lena’s doubt to waft amongst the blood moon colored zephyr like that of an old memory.

    They turned down one corridor of the hedge maze after another, and before Lena knew it, her hand lied empty again— completely absent of Kara’s ghost-like grip.

    Lena spun around in place, confused. “Kara…?”

    Not even the sound of cricket song came back. It was likely that all manner of creatures were quelled into silence by the eclipse, but Kara, however, was just gone.

    Lena’s hands went to smooth over the tight ponytail at the crown of her head. This couldn’t be happening…

    Not again.

    Kara was there one minute and the next she had disappeared. Why did this keep happening? How was it that she was always leaving? The bitter indignation Lena felt for having been left all alone again came clawing back from the fouled and perished parts of her mind. She could feel her heart begin to ache, thudding one unhappy feeling after another…

    The absolute void that Kara had left during the bane of winter… The horrific, swallowing emptiness of it… Beast-like in its hunger to be fed. Parched, ravenous, yet never completely satisfied. Lena dreaded its return more than anything. Begged for it to go away…

    “Please,” She clasped her hands together as if in prayer, “Please, don’t leave me again.”

    The alarm from her phone was all that replied.

    Seven thirty in the morning had arrived in the blink of an eye. More like a single wink, really… Lena felt as though she’d only just laid her head down to sleep.

    “God.” She muttered, staring thinly through the squint of her eyes towards the part betwixt her curtains. She shielded herself from the morning sun with a displeased grunt and simultaneous toss of her bedsheet over her head. If she just went back to sleep, then maybe the day would wait for her?

    …Or maybe she’d just dream about losing Kara again.

     

     

    Thankfully, the day that greeted her was a bit less devastating. There was just something so magical about a late spring morning in Texas.

    A collective sense of understanding went out amongst the vineyard and surrounding town— those who were “glass half empties” had a way of becoming “glass half fulls” in the sweet, airy lull that was the last comfortable glimmer of cool weather.

    Smiles welcomed Lena down each stretch of the winery as she made her way outside. From the attached villa to the courtyard, and all the way to the winery’s tall atrium front, there were people who were happy to see her. Everyone who worked at Shade Tree Cellars seemed to possess a sunny disposition… At least in the springtime, anyway. Summer bore a more serious brow; sweat-dotted and pinched with hard work. With the flux in temperatures between now and then, Lena knew to be ready for faster nods, fewer smiles, and quicker footsteps. It was a good idea to cherish the cheerfulness while it lasted, she decided.

    While Kara was away with her abbreviated crew upon the prickly pear farm every other morning, Lena saw to it that the rows upon the vineyard weren’t neglected in her absence. To her satisfaction, it seemed that the fieldhands kept a steady routine even without their foreman’s instruction. They dropped their pails and baskets full of grape bunches at the end of each trellis ready to wheelbarrow away and would be doing so until later that afternoon. The workers were a well-oiled machine by now, thanks to Kara. Content just to leave them to it, Lena gave a happy twirl of her floral print midi dress back towards the winery. She’d sit inside her office and resume her previous day’s stack of to-do’s, which was (almost always) a never-ending pile of bottle shipments, machinery repairs, and payroll slips. A fine day indeed, despite the sinister foreboding left by her continuing nightmares… She’d no doubt come to dwell on them as soon as she sat down again.

    Prim and proper was the state of her desk. There was her laptop squared upon her large calendar, her two fountain pens to the left and her desk phone to the right… Like always, the small, porcelain cactus figurine (a gift from Kara) was the first object her eyes went for on her way in. What might have been a small, tourist shop trinket to anyone else meant the absolute world to Lena. It represented the hardiness apparent in the woman she loved; The spines with which she protected herself and the sweetness that lay therein. Proof positive that Kara’s fruit was plenty despite the ever-present threat of pain.

    Lying beside the lovely figurine this morning was something new.

    A folded note.

    Curious, Lena plucked it up from her desk with a mind to read it. Jack’s handwriting was there as clear as day, both sophisticated and masculine in its bold capitalization; A stark contrast to her own thin, spidery lettering.

    “Lena,

    I’ve gone to take care of some things. I can’t tell you much more than that right now. The less you know, the better. I’m going to make everything alright again.

    Love,
    Jack.”

    “…Make everything alright again?” Lena said aloud as she leaned her hip onto the carved edge of her desk. “Please tell me you’re not doing anything drastic…” Her phone went to her ear with Jack’s number dialed almost as soon as she could turn around, which then rang, and rang, and rang… but she only managed to reach his voicemail.

    That arrogant tone he used solely for anyone who should so dare to leave him a message made Lena purse her lips with a displeased frown. She called him a second and third time after that, but it seemed that he wouldn't oblige her even to pick up. That, or he’d lost his phone again amidst his chaotic spiraling. Quite the pair, they were… Holding one another up between their own spells of self-destruction. This time, however, it seemed that Jack had all but cut her out. How was she supposed to help if she knew practically nothing at all?

    Lena began to go down the list of people in her mind who she could reach out to for answers, but it had been years since she’d spoken to any of them… The likelihood that Jack owed them money was also very high, so her bringing him up might effectively open more old wounds than she intended. A trip to his apartment in the city might prove useful, she thought, but she remembered that she didn’t have a key. Jack had always been so very private about his personal business, even though he appeared to hold no qualms about imposing himself upon anyone else’s. Especially Lena’s. What was she to do if he’d all but made himself invisible? The man had a penchant for getting himself into uncomfortable situations, and even more so for making himself all but skint in his efforts to get out of them.

    The more Lena thought about it, the more she was sure that it had something to do with his missing gold rings and cufflinks. That, and the fact that he’d apparently sold his Land Rover on a whim. She’d not seen him in his fine tailored suits for quite some time, either. 

    Three days, she decided.

    She would give him three days to show up, and in the event that he didn’t, Lena would exhaust every possible means she had of finding him— no matter what sort of unsightly transgressions doing so would turn up. Jack wouldn’t be throwing himself upon his own sword so soon, not if she had anything to say about it. It wasn’t as if she was unused to his skipping town every so often. He’d return with a sore look about him, either defeated or embarrassed or any number of things that made a proud man feel small, but at least he would return… or so she hoped. For some reason, things felt a bit more ominous this time. Lena couldn’t quite place it. Thinly veiled threats seemed to creep out from the shadows here in the Texas hill country, especially if one were too careless or simply lacking in respect. Either way, it would take a great deal of patience just to let Jack try to handle things on his own.

    Too unsettled to work, Lena thought about visiting the breakfast reservations at the Shade Tree Bar & Grill. Jack was always insisting that she make more appearances outside of an administrative role, anyway… She figured there wasn’t any harm in shooting the breeze with a few guests that morning and set out in that direction with the note tucked away in one of her desk drawers.

    She didn’t so much as make it past the atrium.

    The quiet sounds of sniffling brought her attention towards the easternmost wall of windows, which were nearly just as wide as they were tall, all the way from rafter to tile. Birds had once again made themselves at home in the tucked away angles of each beam up above. Nests with newly hatched chicks, no doubt… but Lena paid them no mind as she searched out the sounds of crying.

    Nia stood ensconced there in the long, floor length drapes as if she meant to hide from the world. Quickly did she wipe beneath her eyes upon Lena’s approach, smudging her neat, winged eyeliner in the process.

    “Nia?” Lena said with a soft voice, “Are you alright?”

    “Fine—” Nia replied, turning her face towards the window’s sunny warmth again, “Just admiring the view.”

    Unconvinced, Lena felt that she had dealt with the pridefulness of the south for long enough. It was time that people stopped lying to her. Or at the very least, shielding their true feelings from her. “Is that why you’re crying? The view?”

    “I’m not crying—”

    The thick, woven material of the drape came away with a sudden swipe, and Nia hid her face further.

    “Well, let’s say that you were…” Lena suggested, “What would make you do that?”

    Nia finally gave her a quick side-eye glance. “Theoretically speaking?”

    “Theoretically speaking.” Lena affirmed.

    Nia sniffled one last time and turned inside of the drapes, still choosing to abscond herself away within them. “Maybe the fact that I might never be good enough to marry…”

    “What?” Lena gave a surprised quirk of her brow, “Who on earth told you something crazy like that? I’ll hunt them down and make them wish they’d never been born…”

    “Lena—”

    “…and God forbid I be in a bad mood that day.”

    “Lena—”

    “I mean, seriously, everyone deserves to be happy—”

    “It was me, okay?” Nia exasperated, her face held in her hands now, “I say it to myself.”

    “You- what?”

    “It’s the truth, isn’t it? Why else wouldn’t he have asked me yet?”

    Lena fumbled, gesturing clumsily in the place of words, “You mean Brainy?” She huffed, still confused, “Wait- are we still speaking theoretically?”

    “Do you think he’s just settling for me? Most of the time I feel like I’m just holding space for someone else to come along, a real wom—”

    “Don’t you dare.” Lena interrupted finally. She took Nia by the back of her head and pulled her in tightly, hugging her as fiercely as she could, “Don’t you ever complete that sentence, do you hear me?”

    Nia’s shoulders went up and down with a deep breath in and out, and then she nodded instead of answering. The sleek row of her dark brown braids ran smoothly into the palm of Lena’s hand.

    “I’m such a cliché.” Nia drawled, “Ever since I was little, all I wanted was to be married. I wanna wear a white dress and walk down the aisle, and dance with the man I love- but it’s like the word ‘marriage’ isn’t even in his vocabulary. I don’t know how else make it clear to him.”

    “Have you tried just… talking about it?”

    “You’d think all the bridal magazines I leave laying around our apartment would give him some sort of clue…”

    “Really, Nia.”

    “For crying out loud, I’m twenty-one years old. I’ve no money and no prospects. I’m already a burden to my parents, and I’m frightened—”

    “First of all,” Lena reared back to look Nia in the eye, stifling her urge to laugh, “You know how I love your flair for the dramatics, but isn’t this something that can be easily fixed if you just talk about it?”

    “When was the last time you heard more than ten words come out of Brainy’s mouth at any given time?” Nia deadpanned.

    Lena tucked her chin then with a short, affirmative nod. “Right, but how do you really expect him to know something unless you tell him?”

    “I—” Nia stopped, “Oh.”

    “Exactly.”

    “I mean, I guess.”

    “It doesn’t necessarily need to be a dialogue. Just get it out there. Let him do the rest.”

    Something inside of Lena burst with light all of a sudden, like a bulb flashing on amidst so much looming dreariness. Kara had confessed her love for Lena surely enough, having shouted it from a literal rooftop— but had Lena thought to verbally return the same sentiment? Words were sometimes a thing that were neither here nor there. Half of the time they made no difference, effectively spat into the wind and carried away with little to no meaning to them, but now, she realized, the one-sided confession had left a rather steep deficit concerning the words which did hold meaning, such as “I love you too” or “You mean the world to me”.

    Lena realized that she hadn’t, in fact, said it back.

    Why?

    I love you too, Kara…

    Was it really so hard?

    She had fallen head over heels for her easily enough, so maybe it was just a thing she’d assumed Kara would gather for herself? How selfish. How silly.

    “So, I’d just,” Nia quirked her chin, apparently more amenable now to the idea, “…sit him down and say it? That I want to get married?”

    “Seems like the best way to go about it.” Lena replied.

    “Okay.”

    Lena took the pads of her thumbs to wipe gingerly beneath Nia’s eyes, taking the pitiful raccoon-ish looking dark circles of weepy mascara along with them. “I think sometimes we forget how easy certain things should be…”

    “Yeah.”

    “Feel better?”

    “A little.”

She hoped that Kara wouldn’t be too tired to spend the evening together, but she wouldn’t blame her if that turned out to be the case.

Kara brought the work truck to a stop right in front of the open bay door and then jumped out with a smile on her face. The kind of smile that said “wow, you look incredible.” She playfully bit the edge of her fist between her teeth then, a sure sign that she’d seen Grease at least once.

    That’s my Danny.

The terrain was rocky and unpredictable where it wasn’t leveled off with ballast, but her flats were a far sight better than having to run in heels. She hadn’t the slightest notion to ask just yet, as it was all she could do just to wrap her arms around him and cry. Lena sniffled in between her frantic hugging of him. Her heart had split in two again.

    “What?”

    He swallowed hard, clearly parched, “It’s not safe, we have to leave. Now.”

    “Hush, it’s alright.”

    Jack only nodded in reply.

    Lena cupped his chin in her other hand and then turned his face so that she might shave it for him. He didn’t argue. He merely stood propped up against the countertop of Lena’s bathroom and allowed her to be as meticulous as she desired. He owed her that much. Lena was patient with him as long as he offered her more and more of an explanation in between the long strokes of her razor.

    “It took weeks just to find a way out- somehow I ended up south of the border.”

    “I can’t believe she held you prisoner like that…” Lena despaired with another clean wipe of her blade against a folded towel. “After this, we’re going straight to the police—”

    “No.” Jack clutched at her wrist, stopping her mid-sentence, “I’ve told you- we need to leave. I can’t be sure if I was followed back here.”

    Lena remembered the men who had accosted her in the parking lot back at the grape festival. A shiver ran all the way through to her hand which terminated at the end of her razor blade. Jack’s eyes flitted there immediately.

    Kara had been standing just out of earshot, picking up Jack’s clothes one at a time up off the bathroom floor. Her eyes instinctively snapped toward Jack’s grip, and she stood back up, attentive— if not protective. Jack had obviously been through an ordeal, but Kara seemed to draw a line at his grabbing of her. In turn, Jack noticed the fire in Kara’s eyes and let go of Lena. He’d not meant to be so rough. Lena had already forgiven him… She was just glad to have him back and in one piece.

    “You’re not listening.” He replied, “Veronica Sinclair is the law. I ran all the way south to Mexico and she still came after me. I was only her guest as long as I was willing to give her the details she wanted.”

    “Which were?”

    “She…” Jack’s clean bottom lip began to tremble. His eyes became wet with tears, “She kept asking me about you.”

    “…Me?”

    “I tried not to—” Jack choked for a split-second, “But she…” His eyes took to the bathroom floor, too ashamed even to look at her, “She made me do things.”

    Lena felt a rage bubble up inside of her. This man, this poor, infrequently idiotic, sensitive, proud, yet wonderful man took up a large part of Lena’s heart— and by hurting him, Veronica had only hurt her.

    She would pay for it.

    “What’s her goal, exactly?” Lena asked.

    “I think she wants an in, something to sink her teeth into in regard to your family’s money.”

    “Honestly,” Lena combed at some of Jack’s hair with her fingertips, “Her fascination with my family name borders on the pathological… You told her how dangerous it could be to meddle in my father’s affairs?”

    “She didn’t care- she’s a psychopath. Danger only applies to the people around her.”

    Kara had drawn closer by then, curious of their conversation. “I’m lost. What does Roulette want with the Luthors?”

    Jack paused to take a deep breath in and out and rubbed at the corners of his eyes. “I take it you’re abreast of the Luthor legacy?”

    “In part.” Kara admitted. “I know Lena’s parents have had shady dealings in the past- my family’s farm nearly being one of them.”

    “Sure.” Jack agreed, “But what you might not know is how ruthless they can be… and how hazardous to one’s health it is to nose into their business. Roulette is nothing compared to Lionel and Lillian Luthor. But, come to think of it, she might share some level of insanity whenever it comes to Lex.” Jack’s expression fell downcast whenever he saw the frown that spread across Lena’s face. “Sorry…”

    “So, what do we do?” Kara asked.

    “We leave.”

    Lena tried to console him again, “Jack—”

    “My darling, I’m so sorry. I tried as hard as I could…”

    “I know you did.” Lena pouted as she thumbed at the healed cut on Jack’s eyebrow.

    He attempted a smile back at her. “After I escaped, all I wanted to do was to come back here- but I hoped that if I could keep her occupied long enough with looking for me, that she might just give up and distract herself with something else.” Jack hung his head again. “She’s relentless.”

    Kara peered at him a bit more closely, “Are you ready to eat?”

    Jack popped his head back up with that, and with quite the vapor, too. “I keep telling you both- we have to go!”

    “Stop, Jack.” Lena caught him by the shoulders once he made a move to get past her, “We’re going to take care of you before we do anything else.”

    “There’s no time!”

    “Nia’s on her way with something for you to eat, she’ll be so happy to see you—”

    “Lena, please.”

    Lena shook her head, “Get it through your head, Jack. I’m not leaving.”

    Jack stopped himself that time from taking her up by the wrists again. He was so very adamant in his fear of being found. “She could show up at any time, I’m surprised she hasn’t already.”

    “Wait,” Kara interjected, “You think she’d just throw caution to the wind and come straight to the vineyard anyway?”

    As soon as Jack nodded, Kara turned on a dime and darted back into Lena’s bedroom. The sounds of pulled drawers and shaken nightstand lamps made Lena follow.

    “What are you doing?” She asked as she rounded the corner.

    Kara shook her head, not bothering to even look back up from her rather clumsy stuffing of a suitcase that was once beneath Lena’s bed. “Packing.”

    “Packing?”

    “Nia can run the show while we’re gone. I’ll come up with something in the meantime to tell my dad—”

    “Kara, no.”

    “She knows how to read the shipping manifests, and—”

    “Stop that!” Lena shouted. She gave a stamp of her foot against the tile for good measure. Lena turned back and forth between her bedroom and the bathroom then to look between the two people she loved most in the world. “Both of you- If I say I’m not leaving, then goddammit, I’m not leaving.”

    “Lena,” Kara dropped the flimsy top of the suitcase closed with an anxious huff, “I’ve lived here a lot longer than you have. I know how this is gonna play out- people are gonna get hurt.”

    “So, what, we just run away with our tails tucked between our legs?” Lena vexed with her arms crossed.

    Kara paused for a moment, and then gave a single, solemn nod. “Yes.”

    “No.”

    Jack tried insisting as well, “Listen to her, Lena—”

    “No! I did not sacrifice this much, and go through all of these pains, and fight my very upbringing just to walk away now!” Lena jabbed her pointer finger out towards the tall, curtained windows of her bedroom, “This is mine, do you hear me? Nobody’s taking anything else from me, ever again.”

    Kara finally dropped her arms at her sides and walked around the bed to stand face to face with Lena. She sighed and then reached down to scoop both of Lena’s hands into her own, kissing the tops of her knuckles with an abundance of patience. “If anything happens to you… I don’t know how I could live with myself.”

    “But you won’t let anything happen to me.” Lena replied, caressing the side of Kara’s face.

    “No.” Kara shook her head, “I won’t.”

    “You promised that you’d always stay by my side. I know you meant it.”

    “I did.”

    “So, we’re in this together.”

    Jack scoffed, apparently still delirious from his journey, “Well. I think you’re both insane- but God help me if I don’t love you for it.”

    The large, sturdy door to Lena’s bedroom opened right about then, creaking noisily upon its old, iron hinges. Nia stepped inside with a gasp, and then gave a shrill, excited screech to follow. The paper bag in her hands fell to the floor.

    “Jack—!”

    The Cost, Too Damn Expensive

    Chapter Summary

    A hand forced, a decision made.

    Chapter Notes

    TW: graphic violence, non-consensual kissing, blood

    Chapter Nineteen: The Cost, Too Damn Expensive

     

    “Nia wants to set up a haunted house down in the cellars.”

    Kara scooted further up to lay her head upon Lena’s shoulder. There went an indecisive “hm” through her nose once she pondered the thought. “It’s a cool idea and all, but probably a little dangerous, don’t you think?”

    “A lot dangerous.” Lena replied. The pillows stacked behind her neck fit just right. They always did in Kara’s bed.

    “I mean, some of those support timbers have rot going all the way through.” Kara added, “A hundred and fifty years is a long—”

    “One hundred and twenty.”

    Kara quickly leaned upward onto her elbow and then dove to give a few tickling kisses near the end of Lena’s jaw. Lena squealed and squirmed to get away, but Kara had her tangled up in the bedsheets tight enough to know that escape was futile.

    Once Lena could speak again past all of her giggling, she rolled closer to Kara and sighed. “Speaking of a long time to go without- when was the last time we ate something?”

    “Mm…” Kara stretched with a yawn, “I dunno. How about some coffee?”

    “Sure. Do you, um, see my clothes anywhere?”

    “Nope.”

    Kara swung her legs out from the bed and stood up without another word, stretching one more time with her arms far over her head. Facing the sun and smiling into it was always the first thing she did every morning… A very small price to pay for a day’s worth of kindness.

    “…You didn’t even look.” Lena pawed at Kara from the bed, missing her just by the fabric of her boxer shorts. 

    “Didn’t I?”

    Lena laughed, “I only have my underwear.” She sat up to root around towards the end of the bed, “Is my shirt still in the living room?”

    “No, the bathroom I think.”

    Kara ducked around to take a peek inside the bathroom door, “Here- this one’s clean.” She tossed Lena a somewhat folded brown work shirt. The now-iconic emblem of Shade Tree Cellars stared back up at her, an embroidered sunrise over the top of a low hill. “Want me to bring it to you?” Kara asked.

    “What?”

    “Your coffee.”

    “Oh.” Lena chuckled as she went to work with each of the buttons on Kara’s shirt. It fit her loosely enough to cover her all the way past her hips if she were to stand up. “Okay then. Black is—”

    “Black is fine, I know.” The sing-song of Kara’s voice trailed off into the kitchen.

    Lena fell back onto the bed then with a smile. Her landing buffeted a few wisp-like objects into the air, drawing her attention past the point of her nose. She squinted to look at them more closely. Cottony little clouds fell down there in a whimsical pattern upon her face. Downy soft…

    Feathers, Lena realized.

    They had fucked the very stuffing right out of Kara’s pillows.

    She sat up in an instant.

    “Kara…?”

    More abrupt noises followed, all of which came from outside Kara’s bedroom door: the shuffling of feet, banging against a pantry door, and a sort of grunting— almost as if someone were moving furniture.     Black jackets. Bald heads. Fists the size of gourds.

They appeared to have a vindictive streak about them; The one holding Kara against the countertop by the back of her head bore a face twisted up with anger. Lena racked her brain for an answer. Her heart seemed to take precedence at the time, thundering loudly behind her ears with a very devastating sense of permanence.

    Something very, very bad.

Jack had been putting it lightly whenever he said that it was “hazardous to one’s health” to meddle in the affairs of the Luthor family.     “I—”

    “No—”

    Veronica leaned downward to seethe more words of abuse directly into Kara’s ear, “You’ll fight for me. I’ll give you no choice.”

    “You’re killing her!” Lena bawled.

    “Give me your answer.”

    “Let her breathe! Look at what you’re doing!”

    A life without Kara began to strobe behind Lena’s eyes like a horror film. She couldn’t bear it— the thought of the sun on her skin for even a single moment in Kara’s absence. How could it do that? How could it shine down upon her regardless of whether Kara existed or not? It seemed so wrong.

They kicked helplessly against the wall there with small, woody -clacks- as she dangled— right up until Kara was wrung away from her like a ragdoll.

She knew that Kara just wished to contain the element of danger to herself and no one else, but Lena wouldn’t have been able to live with herself had she not told Kara’s family. It was just a matter of time.

Lena could tell how unsettled he was. He had every right to be after what he’d survived on his own— most of which he still kept to himself.     “Sam. Oh, my God—” Lena fumbled for one word after another, “How- When—”

    “Jack called me. Which means you didn’t.” Sam replied with a narrow stare. Lena bent down to drag at least two of her bags inside and then pulled her best friend by the hand to stand alongside her. Sam shrugged the small fur collar of her coat around her neck with a shiver. “But don’t worry,” She smiled, “I’ve already forgiven you.”

    “I meant to call,” Lena gestured apologetically with her hands, “I just- Everything’s been so crazy.”

    “Sorry…” He grinned apologetically. Jack and Sam had always tolerated one another, at best. They did so for Lena’s sake. Each of them were her life’s greatest friends for different reasons, with completely different histories.

    Lena cleared her throat then to sweep the apparent awkwardness from the room and made a small gesture towards Nia behind her, “Sam, this is Nia- Kara’s younger sister.”

    “Nice to meet you, finally.” Sam replied with a stretch of her hand.

    Nia took it up with a friendly smile, “Likewise.”

    “…And this is Alex.” Lena waved back towards the couch. Out of everyone within the small space of Kara’s living room, Alex seemed to be stuck staring directly at Sam. She had the blankest of expressions fixed upon her face. Lena had never seen such a reaction befall the eldest sister, so she didn’t quite know what to make of it.

    Alex gulped down a breath at last and then shot her hand out as a means to shake, but then took it back to drop her medical scissors down onto the small tray near Kara’s lap. She stuck her hand out again, this time to swipe her blue examination glove from it at the last second. “Sorry—” She bumbled, “I- um, Alex.”

    Sam seemed to have similar struggles just with getting her feet to move, but was well versed in the way of surpassing an awkward greeting. She took no time at all to correct her poorly timed response. “Sure.” She nodded, both cheeks aglow with a rosy color for some reason. She shook Alex’s hand politely. Featherlight, almost, as if she were afraid to make physical contact. “I mean- Sam. Samantha.”

    Nia giggled. It was no secret that the youngest sister out of the three had a hard time keeping her exclamations to herself. As well as her facial expressions. The one she made all the way from the front door was no doubt a perfect match for what everyone else was busy thinking…

    Alex had probably never been so blown away before. Not since Maggie, whom Lena still hadn’t met, and more than likely never would.

    “How are you feeling, Kara?” Sam asked towards the couch.

    “Like I got the crap kicked out of me, but what else is new?” Kara replied with an ashamed breath.

    “Don’t be so glum. From what Lena’s told me, you’re probably the bravest person I’ve ever had the pleasure of meeting.”

    Kara shrugged. “Thanks.”

    “Well,” Alex cleared her throat clumsily and then stood up to drop the rest of her medical implements down onto her tray, “You’re just in time to help us decide on what to do next.”

    “Oh, well that’s obvious, isn’t it?” Sam slipped her arms out from her coat. She swept her long brown hair over her shoulder and shrugged, “We stand and fight.”

    Nia gave a cheer at that. “I like her.” She said, spinning back towards Sam, “I like you.”

    Lena palmed at her forehead again, anxious as the day was long. “I don’t know…”

    “She’s right,” Kara took a deep breath, “I’ll be the first to admit that it was wrong for me to want to take you away from here. Running’s not gonna solve anything.”

    “Kara, what can we even do?”

    “I dunno, but we’ll figure it out. Together.”

    “But—”

    “Listen to me,” Kara winced again to sit forward just a bit, cupping a few of her ribs with a small, painful wheeze, “None of us are going to let anything else happen.”

    Sam turned around with a nod. “Exactly. I won’t be going anywhere any time soon either, so face the facts, babe. We’re here for you. We’re here for all of it.”

    Lena sighed. “They’re just grapes.”

    “Lena…” Kara smiled up at her again, bruised and beautiful all at the same time, “You know they’re so much more than that.”
