    Pride and Truffle Pigs

    Chapter Summary

    A small and much needed get-away. An army gathers. A goal, attained.

    Chapter Notes

    Chapter Twenty: Pride and Truffle Pigs

     

    PB was a two-hundred-pound potbellied pig.

    She swung her head back and forth whenever she walked; a sort of nimble sweeping motion across the leaf-littered woodland floor. Depending on who you asked, PB had several admirable qualities. She was gentle with children and polite as a rule, liked leftover fried chicken (or any leftovers, she wasn’t picky) and had a mind to do as she was told, but there was one thing in particular that she did which made her incomparable in regard to the rest of her mud-wallowing peers…

    She found truffles.

    Lots and lots of truffles.

    Brainy held fast to the short rope in front of him, trying (and failing, for the most part) to keep PB from breaking out into a dead run. The very large sow had been described as “somewhat overzealous” by the farmer who owned her— a trait made perfectly clear now that they were out in search of the pungent, treasure-like fungi.

    “Why a pig, though?” Lena asked as she walked hand in hand with Kara along the branchy trail, “Wouldn’t it be easier to use a dog- like a bloodhound or something?”

    Kara ducked beneath another low hanging limb of a wide red oak and pulled some of its twiggy, brown foliage away for Lena to follow through. “Sure, I guess.” She replied, “They’re easier to handle, but they don’t root out truffles quite like a potbelly does.”

    “Is that because pigs have a superior sense of smell?”

    “Hm,” Kara rounded out her left shoulder a bit, likely still sore from its prior dislocation, “More like they’re only concerned with a very specific kind of smell.”

    Lena chuckled a bit as she reached out to fix the tan suede vest over Kara’s thin sweater, “It’s not as if she’s looking for a boyfriend…”

    Brainy chanced a split-second to twist around and speak over his shoulder, matter-of-fact as always. “That’s correct.”

    Lena guffawed with her surprise, “…You can’t be serious!”

    Nia suddenly dove towards the wide trunk base of an oak tree once PB began to dig rather aggressively with the flat end of her snout. It took some effort, but Nia managed to muscle her way in front and used a small gardening trowel to quickly unearth a glob-like formation there, maybe from half a foot down into the soft ground. An eager smile scrawled its way across her face as she dusted off the object in her hands. The truffle had come up black out of the ground like a lump of coal and was textured across its entire surface, perhaps the size of a mandarin.

    Nia cheered as she held the expensive tuber away from the gnashing of PB’s teeth. “Good girl!”

    Kara laughed as she turned back to Lena, “Truffles emit the same sort of pheromone as male pigs.”

    “Sex pheromones…” Lena drawled.

    “Mhm.”

    “I’ll admit, that is… slightly disturbing, but also fascinating.” Lena huffed a laugh, “And definitely more information about truffles than I needed.”

    Nia patted the burlap bag hanging at her hip and then shrugged further into her hand-knit sweater, “Kara, are you sure we can truffle hunt here?” She looked around in a manner that resembled anxiousness, “What if a ranger comes by?”

    Kara fingered gently around the corner of her eye nearest her temple. That bruise there had taken the longest to heal on account of the bone beneath it having been fractured. “It’s fine- I’ll just make a quick phone call if that happens.”

    “Yeah, with what cell signal?”

    Kara winced slightly from having furrowed her brow so sharply in response. She shook it off and then took her phone out from her front vest pocket. She nodded, realizing only then just how far out into the sticks they actually were. “Ah.”

    “I’m too pretty for jail, Kara—”

    “You won’t go to jail.”

    “But what if—”

    Kara dabbed at her eye again, a bit more impatiently this time, “Relax. Everything will be fine- That’s why we came out here, remember? To be fine, and to relax.”

    Once Kara’s boots came to a clumsy stop in the middle of the beaten path for all of her prodding at her eye, Lena reached out just to pull her hand from her face. She didn’t wish to make so much of a fuss over Kara’s injuries, knowing the woman’s rather stoic nature and her constantly insisting that she was alright, but sometimes Lena just couldn’t help it. Kara had been beaten so badly… and she had been forced to watch.

    “Agh—!”

    The brown flash of a steel toed boot landed somewhere in the middle of Kara’s gut again, retching a pained gag out from her mouth.

    “No more!” Lena screamed, “That’s enough- please, stop!”

    She could still feel the hot streams of her tears as they had tracked sideways down towards the floor. That was where her head had been pinned, right underneath the dirty sole of another man’s boot.

    “Kara! Kara baby, hold on—”

    Kara’s head lolled back as soon as she was wrenched away from the floor, nearly unconscious now. She hung there limply, eyes swollen and bruised shut.

    “Goddamn you, stop it!”

    A horrific -crack- sound from a set of knuckles meeting Kara’s temple made her fold forward in regret. It was awful, and nearly hard enough to bounce Kara’s head off the peeling linoleum of her kitchen floor.

    “Kara—!”

    “Quit that…” Lena cooed gently as she swatted Kara’s hand away. At least Kara had stopped looking down at her feet every time Lena sought to make eye contact with her. It was understandably difficult… the shame of being witnessed like that. Vulnerable, helpless…

    The swelling around Kara’s eye socket hadn’t done her many favors in full view of the public, but thankfully, most of that was gone now. It was just the faint, blueish remnants of knuckle-shaped dots across that part of her temple and even lower towards her jaw. Lena swept some of Kara’s fine golden hair behind her ear and then gave her a subtle pout, “Do you want to head back to the cabin and rest? How do your ribs feel?”

    Kara took up both of Lena’s hands just to squeeze them tightly against her chest, “Fine- stop worrying. It’s nice outside, I just wanna enjoy the day with you.”

    Nia and Brainy had traipsed further down the path following PB’s steady persistence, which left Lena with yet another opportunity to ask about the goings-on back at the vineyard. It bugged her to no end, Kara’s habitual reticence concerning the subject. At that point, Lena knew very few details. She’d been convinced to agree to such, but now she was regretting it… The “not knowing” was somehow worse.

    “So, there’s no one left at the vineyard, right?”

    “Right.” Kara nodded, fiddling with the tasseled zipper of her vest.

    “Is that because they’ve all gone- or are they just somewhere else?”

    “They’re…” Kara cleared her throat all of a sudden, “They’re somewhere else.”

    Lena sighed heavily through her nose and racked one of her knees straight. “Will you just tell me?”

    “No. You agreed to let me handle it.”

    “Only because you gave me no other choice.”

    “I didn’t want you to fret so much.”

    “Well, that’s too bad…” Lena smoothed her own hair down towards the tie which sat at the crown of her head, “Call me Scarlett O’Hara, I’m fretting.” She was so anxious that she could feel it in her teeth. Begging was still an option, she thought. Damn it to hell, how Lena felt like begging. “Let’s just go back.” She pleaded, “There’s still time to convince everyone to change their minds—”

    Kara took her up again, this time by laying her hands flat against each side of her face. She directed her eyes forward there then, intent upon capturing Lena’s gaze. “I’m telling you right now, none of the workers are gonna be persuaded to abandon you. Not now. I’ve known most of these people my whole life… They won’t hear a word of it.”

    “Fine,” Lena pouted a second time, but a bit more sourly than the first, “…but only if you tell me what everyone’s doing.”

    “Would you just trust me? The whole point of us coming all the way out here was for you to stress less—”

    “I do trust you, but the only thing that’s going to make me ‘stress less’ is knowing whether or not I’m putting everyone in danger!”

    “I’m sorry.” Kara shrugged resolutely, “That wasn’t your decision to make. It was theirs.”

    Lena gripped each of Kara’s hands and then hung her head between them. She felt sick. “Kara… I don’t think I can do this.”

    “Stop. Breathe.” Kara decided just to pull Lena close. She hugged her like that for a long moment while they listened to the wind whistle between the bare branches of the trees that surrounded them. When she finally chose to speak again, her voice possessed a calm tone about it. “They’re meeting at the farm every day to go over some plans- should we get another visit. My dad’s got everything under control for now.”

    “Your dad?” Lena reared her head back, “No, no he can’t be involved. It’s too much—”

    “What, did you really think I wouldn’t enlist every last little bit of help we could get?” The small hill of muscle that sat at the back of Kara’s jaw raised and lowered with the hard clenching of her teeth, no longer accompanied by its dull -click- sound. That had apparently been fixed by one of the many blows she’d taken there. “I would call on everyone at least three counties wide to come and support us, and you know what? They’d answer. Everyone considers you family now. Can’t you feel it?”

    “I- I know, I do.”

    Kara raised both of her arms then to gesture vaguely out towards the wooded area, or the world at large. The hardy oak trees of these secluded woods were few in number compared to all of the stout maples. Lena felt safe amongst so much nature… She knew it was why Kara chose to bring her there.

    “There’s not a single person we know who wouldn’t come to bat for you.” Kara went on, “Everyone who agreed to stay understands the risks involved. They also know that the only way to get rid of Roulette once and for all is to stand together against her.” She pointed towards Lena then with a relief-filled sigh, “You did that, you beautiful, unsuspecting creature, you. You did what they were all too afraid to until now, and I don’t know if it was purely out of spite or if it was your grand sense of determination- but whatever the case, you were the glue that we all needed. Marble Falls owes you a debt of gratitude, Lena, and you already know how we handle those. We pay them in full.”

    Lena felt the sting of tears in her eyes. “I just want it to be over with. I feel out of my mind.”

    “I know, baby girl…” Kara reached out for her again, “Let me shelter a little bit of that crazy, alright? I can take it.”

    There was nothing else so safe and sound like Kara’s arms. Lena fell into them with a sad huff, sniffling past her lingering anxiety. She took a few deep breaths there to settle the panicky feeling inside of her limbs and then craned her head upward to look at Kara again.

    “Thank you.”

    “Sure.” Kara smiled, “And I’ll say it again, you were right to not want to leave whenever I asked you to. I let my fear get the better of me. You didn’t.”

    “That was actually just the spite you mentioned before… It wasn’t bravery or anything like that.”

    “Believe me, it’s all the same in the face of danger. You inspired a lot of people to bring an end to Roulette’s reign. She’s had a chokehold over this place for far too long…” Kara gave Lena a squeeze, “You’ve inspired me, too.”

    “Really?”

    “Mhm.”

    Lena still couldn’t shake the feeling of impending doom from her mind. She shifted her weight back and forth over the brittle, autumn detritus, slinking further inside of Kara’s embrace as if it were the only safe place left in the whole world.

    “You know how sometimes you can wake up from a bad dream and feel that relief that comes with knowing none of it was real?” Lena shook her head against Kara’s vest, “I haven’t felt that in a while now. I wake up and it’s still there… the nightmare.”

    “What else can I do?” Kara asked, “Is it enough for now- being here?”

    Lena nodded. “Yes. Just as long as you keep loving me.”

    “As if I could ever not.”

     

     

    They were a light blue color now, no longer purple and magenta around their edges.

    All of Kara’s bruises played on Lena’s mind constantly, though seeing them up close from the light of their small fire seemed to melt away some of her woe. The calm hearth of their cabin gave off plenty of heat where she and Kara laid upon a large rug— enough so to rid them of their clothes and to bathe in the feeling of one another’s skin. Lena was truly set apart from her stress with each of her repeat climaxes, but even then, some of it returned like a spell she was under as soon as reality came crashing back. It made her desire Kara’s hands upon her like nothing else, reminiscent of their early days together… back when they did very little of talking at all. She’d been so consumed with sex that it had become a crutch, of sorts.

    Was that what she was doing again?

    Running from her problems with the help of consistent orgasms?

    Lena hummed quietly against the warm and heady feeling of the pulse that lived within Kara’s neck. She kissed there sweetly, pawing up and down the slender curve of Kara’s bare hip. “I wish we could stay here forever.”

    Kara huffed a laugh and then groaned to sit up onto her elbow. Her skin seemed to glow there in front of the fire, silhouetted and magnificent like she was some heavenly body plucked straight out from the night sky. First the sun, now a star… She burned so brightly, either way. Lena thought that the steep canyons and butte-like cliff faces of Lost Maples Nature Reserve were incomparable to what lay reposed and glowing right in front of her. Her angelic being, her knight in flannelled armor.

    “What do you think that’d look like?” Kara asked with a warm, sleepy smile, “If we had a different life and lived here instead? In the woods, maybe in this little cabin…”

    Lena draped a leg over Kara’s hip and scooted a bit closer along the shaggy rug, “Well, I would still be a vintner. I don’t think there’s a universe out there where I wouldn’t love making wine for a living…”

    “I think I’d like to be a painter.”

    Lena gasped quietly, “Yes, that’s perfect. You would paint here- in our home, and I’d help you sell off your art in big city galleries with all of my fabulous wine collector friends—”

    “And our kids would take after you, of course. They’d learn how to tap all these maple trees for syrup, or something. What would you call that…?” Kara snapped her fingers beside her head in search of the words, “A syrup wrangler?”

    There went a giggle somewhere in the middle of Lena’s throat. “A sugarmaker.”

    “Huh. Seems fitting, since that’s probably how we made them in the first place.” Kara rolled forward and teased a few playful kisses between Lena’s breasts, “With some sugar.”

    Lena finally cracked a laugh out loud, “You are something else.”

    “Yeah, but I’m your something else.”

    “That you are, daddy.”

    “I want them to have your eyes, too…”

    Lena gave a curious quirk of her chin at that, “Mine?”

    She couldn’t understand it. Kara had the most stunning cerulean irises she’d ever seen in her entire life… Why would their children not be blessed with the very same? How wonderful it would be to make her body home to Kara’s genetics, she thought…

    “Yeah,” Kara gave Lena a few loving blinks in reply, “They could have one eye just slightly different from the other. Barely blue, and then barely green- just like yours. I got stuck there, you know… when we first met.”

    “You did?” Lena blushed.

    It had been so long since someone pointed out the very subtle heterochromia of her eyes. True to style, Kara certainly held the fiercest of her compliments under lock and key, but Lena admired that about her. Where some would gush over her every noticeable feature in an effort to woo her —leaving nothing to delight upon in the future— Kara simply chose the right time and place to make said remarks. Lena felt that she could love Kara for a hundred years, or more, even, and in all that time there wouldn’t be a day that went by where Kara didn’t make her feel like the most singular woman on earth. Forever was so much to look forward to…

    “They made me forget what words were. A few times, anyway. Felt kinda like a bumbling idiot.” Kara confessed.

    “Oh, stop it—”

    “I’m serious!”

    Lena squealed whenever Kara grabbed at her hip to pull them flush from waist to chest. She hummed softly again as soon as they were still. She knew what Kara was doing… Making her think about the future, even a make-believe one for now. It was nearly enough to keep her from dwelling on the present.

    “And I suppose we would keep with tradition and be married with these children?”

    “Oh, absolutely.” Kara replied with a toss of her hair away from her neck, “Mom and dad would be mamaw and papaw, kinda like how they say it around the Mississippi delta…”

    “I thought your parents were from here?”

    “They are. I just like how it sounds, I guess. Like ‘ma’am before maw’. It’s prettier… Sorta sing-songy.”

    “You’ve given this some thought.” Lena said with a sly smile.

    Kara shrugged a little bit with her uninjured shoulder. “Maybe.”

    “So, how far did we plan ahead? Two, three?”

    “Four.” Kara gave a confident tuck of her chin.

    “Four!”

    “Yup. You surprised me with your first pregnancy test on our way to Thanksgiving dinner one year. I cried, you cried…”

    “Let me guess, Alex and Nia compete for ‘best aunt’?”

    “Constantly.” Kara drawled, “But little do they know, they’re both the best aunts in the entire world.”

    Lena rolled over onto her back then and tucked her hands behind her head with a long sigh. “That sounds so nice… I only wish it were true.”

    “Hey—” Kara sat up further and perched herself right above Lena, leaning down for just a moment to peck a kiss into the hollow of her cheek, “Let’s not face the real world again for a little bit. Give me tonight, at least? I don’t wanna see another inch of worry show up on your face.” Kara smiled, “You are so, so beautiful.”

    Lena took another deep breath, pondering the same thought of running once again. It sat at the back of her mind like a godawful itch, unable to scratch it completely away. “I worked so hard for this life, and this wine, and here I want to throw it all away…”

    “Mm-mm. I won’t let you.”

    “No?” Lena turned to face Kara again, “Why not?”

    “Because I love you too much to let you give up on your dreams. That’s not the kind of person I wanna be- Someone who sits idly by while the person they love loses what matters the most to them.”

    “You matter the most to me.” Lena asserted, “You, and this future we’re always talking about like it’s some silly daydream… but it’s real to me, Kara. I’d do anything to make sure you were still there to see it.”

    Kara smoothed her hand over Lena’s hip, trailing her fingertips softly up and then down, “It’s real to me, too.” She kissed gently beside Lena’s ear, “But you can have both. I can give you both.”

    A log split inside the hearth all of a sudden, sending a few glowing embers up into its flue. The sound of crackling fell softly against the returning silence. Did Kara feel some sort of responsibility towards her and this future they played at more and more often? She was as strong-willed and chivalrous as any of the family men back in town —a thing which often made the whole of someone’s personality— but the way Kara’s eyes lit up whenever she spoke of children…? There was so much hope there. It was a sort of joy that seemed to emanate from her, covering everything lucky enough to be within reach.

    “Can I ask you something?”

    Kara took her beautiful blue eyes back towards Lena’s in a flash. “Sure… Okay.”

    “A family of your own is all you’ve ever wanted, isn’t it?”

    A wave of relief seemed to wash over Kara’s entire body. From the top down, she softened as if something very burdensome or heavy had been lifted from her. “For as long as I can remember.”

    “Why have you never just said so?” Lena asked, “Did you think that it would run me off?”

    Kara shook her head against her arm. “No. I just- I dunno. I’m still sloughing off that ‘undeserving’ part of me, I guess.”

    Lena cooed with a brush of her hand against Kara’s chin. “I understand. Will you do me a favor from now on, though?”

    “Anything…”

    “Just walk in a straight line, straight to me. Always.” Lena pressed the side of her face where she could feel the steady beat of Kara’s heart, “If you do that, then everything will turn out just the way you want, I think.”

    “Alright. I’ll do my best.”

    “I know you will.”

     

     

    The cold jaws of winter had sprung upon the vineyard faster than anyone expected.

    It was unseasonably frigid for only November, which was to Lena’s understanding —in the three years that she had now been in Texas— a thing of dark omens for the cold months that lay ahead.

    They were to expect their first frost at the end of the week, and it was all Lena could do to keep from shivering the hardy drop cloth right out from her hands.

    She was worried about the begonias along the front walkway not surviving the cold. Their sad demise the previous winter consisted of blackened leaf ends and withered stems… A sore sight compared to their jubilant days in the summer sun. Their pinks and purples were the crown jewels of the winery’s very prettily landscaped front walk, so this year, Lena decided to be a bit more preemptive.

    Nia was rolling small towers of caged wire along the dying grass to leave lofty areas in between the flowers. She’d leapt at the chance to help, like always. Lena couldn’t remember why they hadn’t just hired the same landscaping company to come back out and overwinter the front walk in the first place… It seemed to be one of Nia’s trademark whirlwind ideas, to take care of the flowers herself. She admired them out loud so often. It was clear that she loved them, the fragile yet hardy things in life. Thought of them as things to care for. She was so much like her sisters in that way… Perhaps nurture really did win out over nature, sometimes. Lena wouldn’t be at all surprised if said nature of all three Danvers sisters were, in some shade or other, just like the hilly wildflowers that spread themselves over the vineyard each spring…

    The love they shared seemed to grow in the most unlikely of places.

    “What are those for?” Lena asked as she scooped some more of the dead foliage out from the flower bed, nodding towards the rolled wire.

    Nia dusted her light blue gardening gloves with no small amount of pride. “They’re tomato cages.”

    “And… what are we doing with them?”

    Nia then pointed towards the bunches of weepy, magenta-colored begonias, “We use them to prop up the frost cloth over the flowers for the winter. The freeze will just transfer straight to their leaves if you leave it lying on top of them… This way they’ve got a better chance of coming back in the spring.”

    “Hm. Okay.” Lena hummed. She untucked her hands from her fleece-lined puffer jacket and began pulling the cloth over the rolled cages.

    “Not there—” Nia waved towards the spriggy, fragrant rosemary, “Don’t worry, that’ll survive a nuclear winter. Trust me. Just the flowers.”

    Lena gave a simple nod and stepped around the sprawling evergreen shrub. “Oh, I’ve been meaning to ask,” She stopped, “…is there any sort of standard for protecting all the cactus back on the farm from the cold? Should I buy, I don’t know, more cloth?”

    Nia shrugged as she draped her corner of the cloth over the bed, “I wouldn’t worry about that. Prickly pear’s sort of indestructible.”

    “Well, that’s one less thing to worry about, at least.”

    “Speaking of flowers and painful things,” Nia stood back up, “I saw a bundle of roses shoved into a trash can outside your office a few mornings ago…”

    Lena stopped.

    “Is everything okay? With—”

    “They weren’t from Kara.” Lena replied quickly. She didn’t want to leave Nia with any reason to suspect any tumult between the two of them. The truth was that they were a gift from Roulette, left upon her desk with a card that read “see you soon”. It did just as much to intimidate her as much as it did to piss her off. They’d gone into the trash with an angry stomp, and she’d all but forgotten about it. “It was, um- a confused vendor.” Lena replied. Lying to Nia tasted awful, which left her with the urge to dismiss herself as quickly as possible. “There’s actually some work I have to get back to, if you could finish up here?”

    “Sure.” Nia nodded towards either side of the flower beds that followed the walkway, “Won’t take me too long—” 

    A black town car rolled into the empty parking space nearest the front doors right about then, choosing the small pavement lot over the gravel-filled guest spots that spanned the rest of the section. The fact that the paved parking spaces were painted with the word ‘reserved’ only told Lena everything she needed to know about who it was that had pulled up.

    The driver exited quickly and then wheeled around to open the door behind them.

    Lionel Luthor stepped out. Tall, bearded, and with beady, disapproving eyes like cooled lava…

    The hairs around his chin were a bit grayer than usual… but at least his brow was set with that same perturbation that Lena recognized. Her father never gave any warning as to his comings and goings— a tactic to keep his enemies guessing, more than likely. He was never in the same city for too long.

    “Dad…” Lena croaked.

    The Luthor patriarch simply nudged his head to the left, directing his daughter towards the tall A-frame entrance of the winery.

    Lena twisted back around towards Nia, who seemed to be stuck ramrod straight and staring at the man as if he were engulfed in flames. “Don’t worry, I’ll handle it.” Lena blinked, “Nia…?”

    Nia snapped herself quickly out of her daze, “Huh?”

    “Are you alright?”

    “Yeah, no—” Nia shook her head, trailing the beaded ends of her long, skinny side braids over her shoulder, “Fine- Go ahead. I’ll do this, you do… that.”

    Lena puzzled her brow a bit further but then resigned to catch up to her father. He’d given no sign at all that he would wait for her. “Okay. Thanks.”

    The sound of her jogging footsteps once they hit the tile echoed high up into the rafters of the tall atrium. A left down the main hall brought her swinging into the open door of her office. Lionel was already craned over the end of her desk, having shoved her green shaded banker’s lamp all the way to the end. There beneath his hands sat a small stack of papers, to which he was scribbling upon one at a time. Lena stepped ever so cautiously forward, silent and absolutely clueless as to whether she was one blink away from being scolded like an unruly child, or something else entirely…

    “It’s good to see you.” She said.

    Lionel waved at her. “Come.” The scowl that sat on his face seemed no more out of place than his usual countenance, so maybe whatever it was wasn’t as horrible as she expected. He jabbed his ornate fountain pen at Lena once she came alongside him, “Sign.”

    Lena took up her father’s pen and squinted down towards the pages where her lamp used to sit. There at the top of the first were the words “statement of transfer”. At the bottom was her name, marked very clearly upon the line designated “grantee”.

    He was signing over ownership of the vineyard. Like it was nothing.

    Lena cleared her throat, knowing full well not to ask questions and just to do as she was told. A simple thank you would suffice where her father was concerned— but instead, Lena felt a great and dire need to hug the man. She’d not done so since she was very little, back when she had clung to his leg out of fear the very moment she had been brought into his home for the first time.

    “Here.” Lionel pointed again, and then again, and then a third time after flipping through to the final page.

    Lena saw it for herself this time, her father’s signature in ink. Two large L’s denoting his legacy in calligraphic form. Had three years really been enough to forget what it looked like? Her own signature felt thin and shaky next to his. She could hardly think straight— so much so that it was a wonder she could even scratch the end of her father’s pen across the paper. Rare was it that he ever deigned to be in the same room as her, and notable were the times that he wasn’t altogether displeased with her…

    This visit appeared to fit the bill on both accounts.

    He gave a gruff hum beneath his breath and then took photos of each page once she was done signing, likely to send off to whatever team of powerhouse lawyers he currently had at his disposal.

    And then, he turned upon the heel of his polished dress shoes to leave without another word.

    Lena’s heart jumped into her throat yet a second time. There he was, the man who was at least half responsible for bringing her into the world, and also for furthering her education, as well as setting her up for success… Leaving. Just like he always did. The privileges that came with being the only daughter of Lionel Luthor were plenty, to be sure, but it was all for naught if she couldn’t have his love. Whether that love was shaped more like approval, or even acceptance, she couldn’t be sure. All Lena knew was that she wouldn’t get it if she so much as told him the truth— that a maniacal loan shark and fight club operator had set her sights upon the very vineyard she’d worked so hard to attain. Now that it was Lena’s in plain, legal writing, said maniacal loan shark (and veritable psychopath) may very well have it after all…

    Her father could help. He would too, just as he had done so in the past for her brother… but Lionel Luthor’s help came at a devastating price. The cost of lives either missing or dead totaled higher and higher every time his children came crawling back to dad. He meant to put a very serious end to conflict if he were to play a hand in any of it at all. The severity of man lieth with him. Lena couldn’t stomach the thought of telling him about Roulette. No, she and Kara (and the rest of the vineyard) would handle the matter themselves. They had amassed a small army in their effort to take back their town, so if they couldn’t do it, then no one could.

    Still, the sight of him leaving without so much as a “goodbye” made her bones rattle to a halt.

    “Dad—” She called out after him. Her feet took her quickly enough to the door of her office where her father stood, his coal black eyes laying fiercely upon her in an instant. “I,” She squeaked, “…I love you.”

    For as stern as the man was in his expressions, the one he bore facing his daughter suddenly turned soft. His brow smoothed at once, and the hard clench of his jaw fell slack. He simply looked at her. No judgement. No sourness. No resentment… His gaze didn’t seem so intimidating anymore. The dark, woody color of his irises flitted to the side of her face where he took his hand and cupped her there… and Lena froze.

    “Well done.”

    Then, just as quickly as he had arrived, he was gone.

    Lena stood in the doorway of her office, short of breath and flummoxed beyond belief. Her father had never said anything so kind before… It was almost as if he was proud of her. Lena nodded to herself then, deciding just to take it as a win.

    He was proud of her.

    She just knew it.   

    Chapter End Notes

    The Viper and the Lamb

    Chapter Summary

    A chase, a stand-off, and an escape.

    Chapter Notes

    TW:
    violence, blood, gore/graphic depictions of death

    Chapter Twenty-One: The Viper and the Lamb

     

    A cellarmaster’s work is never done.

    Lena nosed patiently into a glass to collect the aromatic notes that lay therein, then she sipped with her eyes closed. To dull one sense was to invigorate another.

    Her favorite part of winemaking was that no one vintage ever tasted exactly the same. Vintners much her senior were remarkably skilled at coming close to remaking near perfect wines, but it was still a feat nigh on impossible even for the legends in her field. Jack swore up and down that a strong scientific approach was key for cracking an identical wine. Such was the reason for his insisting upon new casks, lest the oak staves of their reused barrels splinter at their seams altogether. Lena considered the fact that new oak did indeed have a lot to offer a wine— there was definitely a very pleasing vanilla flavor to be had. She sipped from her glass again and continued swirling it around from its stem, enticing the cabernet within to meet the cool, dry air of the underground cellars. 

    At this rate, she just might hire someone to take on the role themselves. The strain in her neck from wearing so many hats was becoming tiresome. She could very well take one day off a week, should she like to, but that was the thing, wasn’t it? She liked working just fine. Everything she dreamed of already lay right there at her fingertips, but sometimes there was just so much of it… At least twenty more casks of various sizes were waiting to be tested just in this staging area alone. The barrels that were upstairs in the viewing room were more fitted for tour guests, since their only use was to display how wine aged in the first place. They didn’t make that heart throbbing, vacuum-sealed sound whenever you pulled their bungs free, for one thing.

    Kara’s favorite plaid shirt was rolled up along each of her forearms, and Lena saw the subtle raising of her small, dark hairs there whenever she passed her nose over the uncorked barrel in front of her. This was why she loved making wine… All of their hard work had culminated into liquid happiness, and if properly cared for at the correct intervals, would make for the grandest of memories. She ran her fingertips appreciatively over the burned cooperage mark upon the barrel’s side and then popped its fresh cork bung back into place with the side of her fist. There were mallets lying around somewhere whose sole purpose was to do just that— but that involved finding one. The whiny pitch of Jack’s voice popped into Lena’s head as soon as she sat her glass down in the low lamplight. She could hear him now, scolding her either this way or that for not sealing each barrel properly. Oxidation was the bane of a winemaker’s existence.

    Lena sighed with a pinch to the bridge of her nose as she turned around to search out one of the smaller mallets. The larger ones held with two hands were plenty in number, as they were laid out at equal distances down both long ends of the corridors leading out of the room. Sledgehammers to re-seat a barrel’s iron hoops, should they need adjusting. They had just been left there… as if they were meant to wait. Lena puzzled her brow left and then right…

    That’s strange.

    A heavy -bang- sound from above racked her with a sudden, hard jolt.

    What the—

    The tunnel’s ceiling had shaken with it, forcing curtains of dust and dirt to fall from the bricked archways on either side of the room. The jarring noise was similar to that of a rolling bay door— one of the few aboveground which sat inside the loading bay. Someone must have slammed it shut. Lena quickly took herself out from the barrel rows and jogged back towards the stone steps to see for herself. Thankfully, she hadn’t ventured too far into the main cellar. Should a tunnel collapse from such a shock, she would have no other choice but to exit far upon the other side near the river. A lengthy three-mile journey just to walk begrudgingly all the way back. There was still much of the cellar network that had not been mapped, making it even more of a danger to anyone not familiar with its ins and outs. Most believed that a great deal of it was used at one point to transport illegally made wine during prohibition, but many of those routes were too dilapidated to traverse now. There was no way to know where it all led. 

    A strangled voice met Lena at the top of the stairs, pleading incoherently as if trapped with no means of escape… and a pair of feet dangled from the catwalk above.

    Lena squinted past her disbelief. She saw more clearly now, though it surprised her to no end.

    Not feet…

    Hands.

    Someone was hanging upside down over the railing. Flailing. Shrieking.

    Lena covered her mouth as she gasped, and before she knew it, the person hanging above her was yanked back over the top rail by the tail of their white coat. They fell flat against the suspended, grated metal walkway then with a -crash-, making Lena leap out from beneath them as if they would fall right on top of her. Looking up through the catwalk now, she could see large shoes stepping near the fledgling sommelier’s head.

    “Where is she?” A voice asked, gruff and impatient.

    Lena stopped breathing altogether. She was standing right beneath him, the man who’d taken to pummeling Kara especially hard until he’d all but wore himself out. Fear mixed with the fire in her belly… because there he was. The perfect object of her revenge. Unfortunately, Lena realized too little too late that the only bullseye between them was the one that was smacked squarely upon her face. It was almost like something out of a Stephen King novel; the way their eyes pulled towards one another. Up, and then down. He saw her, and she saw him. A fraction of a second was all that it took…

    “Down!”

    Running bootsteps began to echo in from all around her. Lena saw at least half a dozen of Roulette’s grunts barreling her way, having poked their way out from between pallets of crates and the odd forklift there upon the ground floor. Come out from the woodwork, they had. They were disorganized and shouldering hard amongst one another, jockeying for position… almost as if they’d been set free from their invisible leashes. Mad hounds they were, seemingly out for her blood. The metal steps of the catwalk gave out their protesting -clang- sounds as more of them raced downward to meet her. There were so many.

    Gone was that wild streak of her anger. Lena was stuck. Paralyzed by fear.

    If not for the hands that pulled her back towards the cellars at the very last second, then she might have even been caught. One of the workers had shoved her behind the tall, oak doors in a flash and shut her up inside the stairwell leading back down. The flat heel-end of her converse missed the edge of the first step, and she stumbled, barely catching herself upon the rickety wooden railing. It groaned pitiably but held her weight well enough. After that, the commotion upon the other side came with one loud crash after another…

    -slam-

    -bang-

    -crack!-

    The double doors jolted heavily against their iron hinges, nearly throwing themselves right out from their solid brick frame. The weight of several sprinting bodies having landed there nearly split the wood in half. There were screams that echoed in from the splinters, and then someone else who shouted at her through all the chaos, “Lena, run!”

    Not so much as a stray thought between her ears, Lena bolted back down the steps.

    All of her preparing and choice words should Roulette come calling had left her without a prayer… Out the proverbial window it had flown, all of it. There had been no warning. Though, Lena doubted very much that she’d have been given any. All of Veronica Sinclair’s shark-like circling had certainly done a number on her. For weeks, Lena had dreaded that cold smile and the way that it might come creeping around the threshold of her office door whenever she least expected it. Did she really mean to take everything by force? Whatever happened to that cruel, sadistic sense of diplomacy?

    In all honesty, the last thing Lena expected was to be chased into submission.

    No wonder the town was in such a stranglehold… Everything was a game to Roulette, and the woman did anything but lose.

    Lena hit the bottom stonework landing of the stairwell with a skid and kept pulling her feet forward. Behind her, the doors at the top of the cellar stairs had finally come apart and out from them poured too many pursuers to count. Lena dared only once to fling her eyes over her shoulder as they raced down the steps after her. They tore around one another as they each hit the ground running. All of the smooth river stone and brick worked well to keep the underground cellars insulated throughout the seasons, maintaining a level sixty-five degrees even during the winter, but a fire raced across Lena’s skin with her indecision on where next to run, or which turn to take. She pulled a barrel trolley behind herself as she ran past, yanking it hard to obstruct the middle of the tunnel. The pack behind her collided with it seconds later, and crashed on top of one another with an amalgam of pained shouts and curses. Her quick thinking paid off. They had gained on her so quickly, like hell itself nipped at their heels.

    The sharp edge of a brick sliced the base of Lena’s thumb open as she turned a corner, but the pain was almost unnoticeable for how frantic she was. She’d bought herself an opening, at least. There was time enough to make another split-decision turn, hopefully to throw the men behind her off her trail completely. The tunnels grew more dark with less and less space— with no rooms to flood with hardy lamp light, the skinny corridors seemed to stretch on for days. Miles they went underground, spanning the whole of the vineyard right up to the red, muddy banks of the Colorado. Lena listened for the hurried stomping of boots behind her, focused on nothing else until someone caught her mid-sprint and pulled her into a darkened niche between the brick. A hand suddenly clapped over her mouth to cease her terrified screeching.

    Blue eyes the color of sunlit glaciers stared back at her, and she stopped.

    Kara held a finger up to her lips, “Shh…”

    Oh, my God—

    The iron head of a sledgehammer hit the floor between them with a -thunk- as Kara pressed them further into the niche. Lena felt the shallowed breaths from her nose run over Kara’s fingers, and in a matter of seconds, the running gaits of several angry henchmen ran right past them.

    Only one slowed to a stop behind the rest.

    Kara took her hand back from Lena’s mouth and then leaned carefully to peek out from behind the darkened cover of the crag. The man turned in place several times, gnashing his teeth and squeezing his hands into fists. Lena could see the long stretch of his shadow from the far away stringed lamps that continued down the tunnel. Suddenly, his shadow doubled back. Kara flung herself out of sight just in time.

    “Kara- I’m scared.” Lena whispered. She was shaking from head to toe, too frightened even to breathe too loudly.

    “It’s okay, everything’s gonna be okay…” Kara hushed against the side of Lena’s face, “I need you to get out of here.”

    “What? No—”

    “Listen to me. We can handle these guys- but not if they get to you first.”

    “We? Wait a minute, Kara—”

    “Head for the storm water pipe. Find a way to drop down inside and follow it all the way to the river.”

    “Baby, please…” Lena began to drop shaky tears from her eyes, one after the other. The footsteps drawing nearer to them began to grow louder and she squeezed Kara with all her might, “Please, come with me.”

    “I love you.”

    “…Kara!”

    Before Lena could so much as stop her, Kara took up her sledgehammer handle in one hand and plucked half a brick up from the ground with her other. She whipped the molded clay chunk hard into the tunnel, throwing herself out from the niche behind it. True as always was her aim. A pained yawp traveled back, and then Kara did the unthinkable…

    She swung the hammer end of her sledge against one of the load bearing timbers, and down the wall came, caving in all the way from the top before completely folding in on itself. One swing was all it took. The tunnel collapsed downward with a loud and dusty -crash-.

    “No!” Lena screamed, “No—!”

    Nothing spurred her onward like the thought of Kara being trapped beneath so much rubble. Lena ran back the way she came and took the next available left to find her way around, hoping to God that Kara had managed to get away safely.

    “I’ll take care of it.”

    Those five words had never rang more loudly in her head than they did right now…

    The drainage pipe that spat itself out along the south bank of the Colorado river was her only option. She knew that it carried on from the cellar tunnels and then connected to a lane of reservoir pipes that lay beyond. She’d never run so fast before. Not a single intrusive thought wormed its way into her mind the whole time her heels kicked out from behind her. She didn’t stop once; the adrenaline coursing through her veins wouldn’t allow it.

    Harried and soaked through with sweat, Lena winced at the bright flash of daylight once she forced her way out from under the creaking, metal grate that capped the drainage pipe. She gripped the soft flannel of Kara’s shirt tighter around her neck as the hard whip of December wind practically slapped her across the face. Huffing one determined breath after another, she climbed her way out from the slippery limestone riverbed. In a certain light, the shallows of it did look a lot like marble when wet— slicked with the almost translucent blue-gray veins of chert.

    A shiver ran down her neck from the cold, and the soft squish of her canvas shoes eventually met dry ground. Lena finally stopped long enough to look up into an empty winter’s sky. Her heaved exhales ran in evenly timed clouds there…

    …and then someone laughed.

    Still dizzy from all of her sprinting towards a mere pinprick of light, Lena whirled around. She couldn’t believe it, her blood all but ran cold… That damned, prideful grin gleamed upon Roulette’s face, toothy and fanged like the awful snake that she was.

    “So predictable, little lamb. Run straight for the slaughter.”

    Lena growled angrily through her teeth. Any animal could pose a threat when cornered, even a lamb like herself. “You stay away from me!” She shouted.

    “Come now, let’s be adults about this.”

    “Says the woman who sends her dogs ahead to do her bidding—”

    “My boys are obedient. They do as they’re told.”

    “Are you sure?” Lena huffed, “Because they looked pretty wild to me—”

    Roulette took another step forward, and Lena shouted at her a second time.

    “Stay back!”

    “How can I when you have what’s mine?”

    “It isn’t yours, goddammit! That’s my life back there.” Lena snarled, throwing her fist in the direction of the vineyard, “Everything I love, everything I’ve worked so hard for- No, I won’t let you take it.” She shook her head and then racked her spine with a ramrod straight posture, “You’ll have to kill me first.”

    “Darling…” Roulette patted the gaudy fur collar of her coat and then swept her other hand near her hip.     “Of course!” Sam waved flamboyantly at herself from the neck down, “Haute couture, darling. What’s not to love?”

    There went Lena’s brow into the palm of her hand again. “Dear God.”

    “Oh- look!” Sam leaned out from their secluded corner again and pointed back towards the bar, “There’s Brainy! Is- is he wearing a tie?” Her brow puzzled, “Do chefs wear ties?”

    Lena spurred them towards the crowd again, whispering quietly at Sam’s back, “Go- go- go!”

    Kara was waiting for them with Alex at the far end of the bar. It seemed that everyone’s favorite blonde had all but run Sara and her tall bombshell of a sister off. Sam skipped her way happily towards Alex and slipped her a hand to hold, and Kara stretched her arm out for Lena to stand alongside her. Lena bathed in the faint honey scent of Kara’s skin as she slotted her arms around with a hug. Kara was quite dapper for that early afternoon, Lena had to admit. Truly, she was the most handsome and earthly creature to ever walk upright on two legs. Her short-sleeved button down had a crisp collar, and she even wore her leather-banded wristwatch, a rarity in itself for the danger it posed while she rummaged around the inner workings of large machinery.

    There stood Brainy in the middle of the room, forehead dotted with sweat beneath the low hanging chandelier. Nia looked up at him with her hands folded primly in her lap. She fiddled nervously with the hem of her skirt, and her knees almost knocked together.

    Then, Brainy got down on one knee. A small ring box appeared in his upturned palm, to which he then opened.

    Even more surprising than that, he opened his mouth.

    “Nia Nal, it has been my absolute pleasure to love you and to be at your side these past years. To cook with you, to wake to you each morning, and to lay next to you each night. To learn with you, and to grow with you. To adventure with you…”

    Lena suddenly felt Kara’s hand grip her own. Her bright blue eyes stared straight ahead towards the middle of the room, but she was rock solid like a monolith inside the eye of a storm. The look of surprise on her face…

    Priceless.

    “I love the way you hum whenever you taste something that you like, and the way you chew around your smile. I love how fiercely you love me, and how loyal you are to your friends, and even more so, your family. I can only hope that I would be lucky enough one day to have a family like that. Daughters. Granddaughters. I know how long of a journey that might be, but would you consider starting somewhere with me? Would you marry me, and maybe see how it goes from there?”

    Nia dropped her mouth open with a single, surprised sob. Her eyes were wet with tears, and they blinked nowhere around the room but at him. Shakily, she took her chin to nod up and down.

    “Okay.”

    “Yes?”

    “Yes- yes!” Nia dove from her chair and wrapped her arms as far as they would reach around Brainy’s shoulders.

    The room exploded with a cheer.

    Lena clapped so much along with everyone else that it took her a moment to look beside her at Kara once again. She was smiling from ear to ear; one tear tracked in a straight line over the buoyant hill of her cheek. Lena leaned upwards onto her toes to plant a quick kiss there at her chin, and Kara took her thumb to wipe beneath her eye.

    “Damn. That’s gonna be a hard act to follow.”

    Lena froze once she dropped back down to her heels. The ecstatic din hovering over the bar had made it hard for her to hear. “…What did you say?”

    Kara turned to smile at Lena. Glacier blue were her eyes when wet, just like always. “Don’t worry.” She replied, “I’ll take care of it.”

     

    —The End—

    Chapter End Notes

    I know that Caddo Lake Whoopsie Daisies left some pretty big shoes to fill concerning my Supercorp stories, but thanks for following along with this one anyway. Left a little room to explore, like always, should I come back to this AU.

    Stay tuned for an epilogue a little later today. If you made it all the way here, then you'll want to read it. Trust me.

    Thanks for reading <3

    Epilogue

    Chapter Notes

    Chapter Twenty-Three: Epilogue

     

    —Three Years Later—

     

    Of course, there was more of that subtle, southern snark of “you’re naming him after your ex?”

    To Lena, it wasn’t as simple as that.

    Fielding more unsolicited advice the second time around was somehow more surprising than the first. It was things like “Waiting to have more is also good” and “Another? So soon?” and “You’re sure it’s not twins, again?” that really made her want to double-down on her adopted southern sass.

    A simple “bless your heart” sufficed in most cases.

    She was just so goddamn uncomfortable all the time. Feeling the size of a house with swollen ankles and walking around in the Texas heat wasn’t good for anyone. Her, Kara, or the baby.

    Their third.

    Jeremiah Jackson Danvers.

    After having named their first two after the most beloved women in their lives, Eliza and Beth, it only made sense to name their third in the same manner— for the men that they cherished. Lena, however, was not about to name her son after her own father. The relationship they had (or rather, the utter lack of one) did not a namesake make. In fact, she was more than happy just to shed herself completely of the Luthor name. Taking Kara’s couldn’t have happened faster, in her opinion. There in the hilly rows of their cabernet grapes —the ones that had brought them together at the very beginning— Kara had tied a ring to a vine one spring and waited for Lena to come upon it. Once she had, she ran all the way back to the villa with the wind in her hair just to say “yes”.

    The twins were just over year old by now. Two little blonde headed girls with blue eyes and a curious streak a country mile wide between them. They liked to sit in Kara’s lap atop a tractor, one to each leg and giggling along with the rumbling sounds of the engine. In just over a months’ time they would have a little brother to teach and to protect, and to be vastly annoyed with, if any of the surrounding opinions concerning “siblings” held any truth at all. The good southern hospitality of Marble Falls was, at the very least, in no short supply…

    Eliza was at the vineyard almost every day. To dote, to fuss, to cook.

    Kara’s mother loved babies. At times, Lena was sad to think that her own mother —her birth mother, Elizabeth— would never gaze upon the smiling faces of her grandchildren, being gone for so many years now, but for what she missed out on, the Danvers matriarch made up for in spades. Eliza would arrive soon for the baby shower they had planned that day. Lena knew this because they had scheduled it for 2pm, so, naturally, that meant Eliza would show up at noon on the dot to “help set up”. Lena was never annoyed by her mother-in-law’s sometimes overindulgence. Besides, she would need all the help she could get with a baby on each hip, and one on the way.

    With Jack in recovery from his gambling addiction and in better spirits as of late (being named the godfather of Lena’s first-born son having much to do with that) he was now moved into the foreman’s house on the top of the hill— his bachelor pad, of sorts. He would no doubt be the first of many to show up to the celebration that day.

    Lena stood in front of the floor length mirror of her old bedroom inside the villa, holding up a slate green, off shoulder maternity dress. It was ruffled below the neckline and would hopefully deter any attention from the size of her ample bosom, but she also liked how it obliged her pregnant belly so comfortably after trying it on for the first time. Excited to dress for the occasion, she spun around and spoke to Kara just outside the French doors that split the sitting area from their bedroom.

    “Daddy, would you come here for a minute? I want to see if you have anything to match this color—”

    Kara appeared from the bathroom all of a sudden, toothbrush tucked into her cheek. “Hm?”

    “This color.” Lena lifted her dress with a smile.

    “Uh, I think my dad has an easter tie like that.” She ducked back in to spit into the sink. “I can ask.”

    “Perfect. Maybe call mom to see if she can bring it with her- I’m sure she’s close to being on her way, anyway.”

    Kara stepped out again and leaned against the bathroom’s doorframe. She crossed her arms and took a slow breath then, as if to prepare for a reaction. “Yeah, she’s already here.”

    “Oh, really?”

    “Mhm. She brought Nia.”

    Lena felt her heart give out a great and awful flutter at that— or perhaps it was just little JJ scooching around a little further below. Some of the leaps he made sometimes were a bit like kung-fu fighting, if she was being honest. He seemed to be so in tune with her emotions already. Kicking whenever she was upset, dancing whenever she was happy, and all but doing somersaults whenever she was thrilled. Lena dropped her dress over the end of the nearby couch and took herself quickly towards the large doors that led out into the hall, still wearing nothing but her ivory slip.

    Kara stepped directly into her path just to catch her on her way out, “Woah, relax.” She brushed some of Lena’s hair out of her eyes, “She’ll still be here by the time you’re done getting ready.”

    “I just want to say ‘hi’ first—”

    “Hey, it’s alright.”

    “But—”

    “Babe, look at me?” Kara took Lena’s face into her hands and then took another deep breath, insinuating that Lena should do the same. Upon their shared exhale, she spoke in a lower register in an effort to exude calmness. “She’s not gonna just up and leave again without seeing you first. I promise.”

    “You talked to her?” Lena felt her nerves settle a bit.

    Kara nodded.

    It had been three years now since Nia and Brainy had packed their things and left in the middle of the night. The timing of it stuck in Lena’s mind like a red-hot poker. She and Kara had announced their eagerness to begin fertility trials, and the next morning… Nia was gone. No word of her leaving. Just… nothing. Eliza and Jeremiah had at least been privileged enough to receive postcards here and there, and a wedding photo to boot, but Lena had called and called until months turned into years, and then finally, she was so far put away from her heartache that she was able to move on at last— but God, did it hurt. Nia knew better than most as far as Lena’s feelings on the subject of abandonment, and the sense of betrayal that it often left her with. She just couldn’t wrap her head around it for the longest time.

    Why?

    What had Lena done that was so unforgivable?

    What hurt the worst was that Nia hadn’t even deigned to come back to enjoy the births of her nieces. What was so much more important than them? It gave Lena more reasons to be angry, but after this long, all she truly felt was excited to see her again. She had so much to tell her. Her mind was a vortex on where to even begin.

    Jackson made a great leap inside of her again, and Lena gave out a small jolt with it. So much for daydreaming with this one; he never seemed to let her stray too far from the present. Kara laughed and stooped lower to speak directly towards him, placing her palm over the oddly timed ripple of Lena’s pregnant belly, “Something tells me you’re not the ‘suffer in silence’ type like me and your sisters.”

    “Oh, please. You do not suffer.” Lena scorned with a playful -tsk- from the back of her teeth.

    Kara merely gave a teasing glance upwards and then put her hand to her mouth as if to whisper, “Actually, I wouldn’t be surprised at all if you had your other mom’s temper—”

    Lena chose to give a smart -thump- to the side of Kara’s head then, who stood erect again with a chuckled “ouch”. From across the room and inside the low-walled playpen there came two matching laughs, similar in pitch but also possessing just enough of a noticeable difference for Lena to tell them apart. Eliza and Beth clung to the padded rails of their playpen and bounced up and down from their wobbly knees. They loved to see their mothers interact with one another. Ever since they were born, Kara’s playful side had manifested exponentially— so it was a frequent sound to bound around inside the walls of the villa on a daily basis: their daughters’ laugher. Kara liked to joke that now that they were walking, it would only be a matter of time before they were running. Lena’s heart raced at the mere thought. The twins were, in fact, so much like their other mother. Oh, the bruises and scrapes in their future…

    “Can you just imagine the sorts of games we’d be playing if Nia had been the one to plan the whole thing?” Kara said once she tucked in the tail of her shirt, twisting there in front of the mirror. “Mom says she’s gonna stay at the farm for a couple days- I figured we can let the girls stay over too. Let them get a chance to really spend some time together… How does that sound?”

    “Yeah, we’ll—” Lena cleared her throat as she took up her dress again, trying hard to mask the hurt sound in her voice, “We’ll talk about it.”

    There went the overabundance of her hormones again, foisting tears into the corners of her eyes. What were the chances that Nia even wanted anything to do with her children? If being gone for so long had been so damn easy, then why on earth would she want to stay, if not for them?

     

     

    Little did anyone know that apart from being able to string more than one sentence together along at a time, Brainy was also quite adept at singing.

    Even though they were lullabies that not a single soul in the place seemed to understand (except for Nia) his voice was soothing enough to settle a very fussy one year old Eliza— who was at least two kicks away from an absolute meltdown. He’d simply taken her into his arms, walked away, and started singing.

    The baby shower for JJ was a much smaller gathering than the first one had been for the twins. Keeping up with two toddling Kara miniatures and orchestrating a party was as much of a whirlwind as expected… Lena was glad to have done it, but she would be even more glad to get off her feet again. With most of the guests now gone and the grill swept clean of paper streamers and spent balloons, Lena went around with a sleeping little Eliza inside the crook of her arm and with her other, plucked one paper cup at a time off the bar top on her way to the trash. 

    A quiet -clank- came from behind the swinging double doors of the kitchen, and Lena stopped. Eliza was still out like a light. Like that of her sister, she could practically sleep through an entire rodeo and not wake up once. Lena walked behind the bar and gently shouldered her way through the doors to see what was on the other side.

    There stood Nia, saucepan in hand and looking down towards the cooking range with a reminiscent stare.

    “Hasn’t been the same without you.” Lena said.

    Nia turned around and pawed at the stainless-steel dish further, hugging it close to her chest. “Kara mentioned that you’ve gone through a few chefs.”

    Lena gave a stiff raise of her brow to confirm, “That, and the line cook we had last was… sort of a disaster.”

    They stood there apart from one another for a moment in silence. One woman to each side of the kitchen. Lena couldn’t stand it anymore— Nia hadn’t hardly said anything at the party and here she was, shut up as tight as a clam again. And for what? More so than it pained her to have been without her for so long, Lena just wanted to know why.

    Dammit, say something.

    She shifted Eliza where she still lay asleep in her arms and then took a few steps more across the kitchen floor. “Where have you been, Nia?”

    Nia’s eyes darted away from Lena in an instant, filled to the brim with guilt. She reached up to tuck her dark brown bob behind her ear. Her hair had been cut so short. Gone were her usual braids and colorful feathers and beads. There pinned near the part on her side was a light blue barrette. It matched the blue color of her eyeshadow, at least…

    “Mom said she showed you my postcards—”

    “I’d rather hear it from you.”

    Nia turned her face this time towards the back of the kitchen.

    “Why won’t you look at me?” Lena felt like crying again. The small warbling in her voice was unmistakable. “What the hell did I do?”

    That got Nia looking at her again. Her eyes went wide, and she shook her head, “You didn’t do anything.”

    “Then what? You’ve been gone for years- What on earth made you just leave everyone like that?”

    Eliza stirred all of a sudden with an uncomfortable gurgle and soon settled again. When Lena looked back up, Nia had her hand covering her mouth to keep herself from sobbing.

    “I did something…” Nia puled weakly from behind her shaking hand, “Something horrible.”

    Lena took another few steps closer and furrowed her brow together with confusion. “I don’t understand—”

    “It was all I could do just to make it one day at a time, facing you, and Kara, and everyone else, but whenever you said you were gonna start trying for a baby—” Nia fought to blink the tears from her eyes, “I just couldn’t anymore.”

    Lena’s heart began to flutter rapidly. “Nia, what are you talking about?”

    Nia stepped forward to close the distance between them and went to shakily brush her hand over the tight, blonde curls that sat upon Eliza’s crown, but she stopped herself at the very last second and covered her mouth with another sob. Her words came out muffled from between her fingers as she whispered, “I killed her.”

    “You…” Lena froze, “What?”

    It was as if the world stopped spinning right beneath their feet. Whoever it was that was standing in front of her was a complete stranger, even though she wore Nia’s flamboyant nail polish and bright hair accessories… Lena simply didn’t recognize her. The Nia Lena knew from before was always so bursting with life and wonder, and could make anyone remember to stop and cherish the small things…

    But this person?

    Trembling. Pallid. Racked with a kind of shame that seemed to dull even the shine of her hair.

    “I killed her.” Nia swallowed hard, “Roulette.”

    Lena shook her head, outright refusing to believe such a thing. “That- no… I mean, you couldn’t have. You were here when—”

    “God, you know—” Nia huffed a disconsolate breath, “I thought that he would end up taking care of it some other way. Run her out of town, frame her somehow… I just didn’t think that he would do that.” She stumbled sideways a bit to lean against a long prep table. She seemed so soul-sick, so tired. Nia hung her head then, appearing as though it took a great deal of weight from her shoulders just to have said the words out loud. “He even thanked me.”

    Lena’s chest began to ache. Damn her heart for the way it wanted to split in half. “Please tell me you didn’t…”

    Nia suddenly furled her hands into fists and raked her reddened eyes back up from the floor, “I hated her.” She sobbed again through the clench of her teeth, “I hated them all- everyone who had ever hurt me, and my sisters, and the people that I cared about. No one ever did anything about the bad people. Ever. They just got away with it- time and time again. I wasn’t gonna let that happen anymore. I wanted to hurt her, but you have to believe me, Lena, I didn’t know- I didn’t know he would—”  

    Lena quickly moved to make her shoulder available for whenever Nia folded forward from all of her weeping. It broke out of her like a dam caving in on itself, floodgates ripped from their hinges and all. Nia’s arms went to wrap around her, and her voice came out as a squeak in between her guilt-ridden crying, “I wish to God that I hadn’t, I can’t live with myself—”

    “How did you even…?”

    “For such a powerful guy," Nia sniffled, "...your dad’s not all that hard to get in touch with.”

    Lena was shocked, of course, but things were slowly starting to make sense. She never understood how Veronica’s men could just turn on her like that. That, and the fact that none of them seemed to possess enough brainpower to be able to handle a shot like that, let alone handle a gun more complicated than a 9mm. The police hadn’t found any evidence pointing to the contrary, so the case had been closed posthaste. It was just like her father to leave no trace of his involvement whatsoever. Lionel Luthor’s near-surgical methods were beyond reproach as far as his peers were concerned. Mad billionaires, the lot of them.

    “Honestly, if you hadn’t, then I wouldn’t be here.” Lena lifted Nia’s gaze by the underside of her chin, “She would have killed me. You have to know that. But as for how it happened, that wasn’t your fault—”

    “Wasn’t it?” Nia sniffled, “She died, Lena. I took somebody’s life away—”

    “No. You didn’t do that. My father did- and believe me, he’s not losing any sleep over it.”

    More than likely, Lionel had hired somebody from his staff to make the trip and to resolve the issue as cleanly as possible. In and out. Over and done with.

    “I still can’t stand myself most days. I feel like a monster.”

    “Please don’t say that…” Lena brushed at the wet surface of Nia’s cheek, “You’re the opposite of a monster. You’re my friend. My sister.”

    Nia looked down and fiddled anxiously with the ring on her finger.

    “Does Brainy know?” Lena asked.

    “He does.”

    “I wish you had just told me…” Lena hoisted the baby in her arms a bit higher, “You wouldn’t have had to miss so much.”

    Nia pulled the glistening dark brown of her irises towards little Eliza again. “I didn’t know what else to do. How could I pretend and act like everything was fine, and lie to somebody so small and innocent? It was so hard just keeping it a secret from you—”

    “I’ve missed you so much.” Lena’s chin quivered hard.

    “I’m sorry…”

    “Please, you have to stay.”

    “I’m a mess, Lena. I was thinking about turning myself in.”

    “No—!” Lena’s heart went into her throat again. She swallowed it back down with a forced gulp and searched frantically for some reason to keep Nia from doing any more harm to herself. “No, please. I- I know how hard it is for you to keep secrets, especially something awful like this, but you can’t tell anyone else. Maybe Kara, and Alex, but please… I’m begging you.” Lena felt a tear leap out from her eye, “I just got you back.”

    Nia shook her head. “I can’t go on like this, it’s killing me. I can’t eat. I can’t sleep…”

    “Then talk to me about it. Me, and your sisters. We’re here for you- I swear. I don’t care if its selfish of me to ask you to not go to the police. I won’t let you throw your life away because someone else took it upon themselves to do something terrible.”

    “Lena—”

    “Stay for me,” Lena begged, nodding towards her daughter, “Stay for her,” Before Nia could shake her head again, Lena took up one of her hands and placed it flat against the round of her belly, “Stay for him.”

    Nia’s eyes blinked upwards at Lena one more time, a little less of that hurt now swirling around within them. “Okay.”

    “Okay?”

    Nia nodded. Lena immediately sent out a breath of relief and then pushed herself forward to dump Eliza straight into Nia’s arms. Nia caught the sleeping baby with a surprised “oh” out from her mouth, but then smiled at the fact that Eliza didn’t so much as wake, not even for a second. Nia sniffled past her few remaining tears and spread her now recognizable smile wider upon her face. A hint of pride sat there. Joy, even.

    “So,” Nia said, “You’re gonna eventually name one after me, right?”

    Lena huffed a laugh, wiping away at her eyes, “Of course.”

    Chapter End Notes

    That's it, folks! I'll be spending the rest of the summer at the lake and working my tail off to hopefully buy a new truck. Gonna keep my old one to go back and forth to the river with my dog, but something new and shiny is definitely in the cards. I've got some ideas swirling around for another Supercorp story, another AU but something that borders upon fantasy/sci-fi... You'll just have to wait and see!

    Be good, now. Tell your mama'n'em that I said hi.
